Hence it suffices to construct a single
\overline{\beta}\in\mathbb{G}_{n}
that satisfies

\|\beta-\overline{\beta}\|_{L^{2}}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log K_{n}}{K_{n}}}\right).

(A.10)

We consider a specific
\overline{\beta}
as a local spline approximation, i.e. the quasi-interpolant (Chapter XII,
de Boor, 1978
): For each
k=1,\dots,K_{n}
, choose an arbitrary
\tau^{(k)}\in\text{supp}(B^{(k)})
, and define

\overline{\beta}_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}B_{t}^{(k)},

(A.11)

which satisfies
\overline{\beta}\in\mathbb{G}_{n}
by construction, and it remains to bound
\|\beta-\overline{\beta}\|_{L^{2}}
. The local support property of B-splines (Appendix
A.1
(iii)) implies that, whenever
B_{t}^{(k)}>0
,
|t-\tau^{(k)}|\leq|\text{supp}(B^{(k)})|\leq(d+1)H_{n}\asymp K_{n}^{-1}
.

We denote by
\beta^{c}
the continuous component of
\beta
, and
\beta^{j}=\beta-\beta^{c}
collects the jumps:

\beta_{t}^{c}=\beta_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}b_{s}^{\beta}ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{s}^{\beta}dW_{s}^{\beta},\qquad\beta_{t}^{j}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\delta^{\beta}(s,x)\mu^{\beta}(ds,dx).

(A.12)

Let
\overline{\beta}^{c}
and
\overline{\beta}^{j}
denote the corresponding quasi-interpolants (on the same knots) of
\beta^{c}
and
\beta^{j}
, and thus
\overline{\beta}=\overline{\beta}^{c}+\overline{\beta}^{j}
by linearity of
Eq.

A.11
. By the triangle inequality,

\|\beta-\overline{\beta}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\beta^{c}-\overline{\beta}^{c}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\beta^{j}-\overline{\beta}^{j}\|_{L^{2}},

(A.13)

and we bound the two terms separately.

We start with
\|\beta^{c}-\overline{\beta}^{c}\|_{L^{2}}
. Define the modulus of continuity of
\beta^{c}
:

\omega_{\beta^{c}}(h)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}s,t\in[0,T]\\
|t-s|\leq h\end{subarray}}\|\beta^{c}_{t}-\beta^{c}_{s}\|.

(A.14)

By the convexity of the norm and
\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}B_{t}^{(k)}=1
, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\|\beta_{t}^{c}-\overline{\beta}_{t}^{c}\|\leq\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}(\beta_{t}^{c}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}^{c})B_{t}^{(k)}\right\|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}\|\beta_{t}^{c}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}^{c}\|B_{t}^{(k)}\leq\omega_{\beta^{c}}((d+1)H_{n}),

(A.15)

and thus

\|\beta^{c}-\overline{\beta}^{c}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sqrt{T}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\beta_{t}^{c}-\overline{\beta}_{t}^{c}\|\leq\sqrt{T}\omega_{\beta^{c}}((d+1)H_{n}).

(A.16)

To calculate the asymptotic order of
\omega_{\beta^{c}}(h)
, we decompose
\beta^{c}=A+M
with

A_{t}=\beta_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}b_{s}^{\beta}ds,\qquad M_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{s}^{\beta}dW_{s}^{\beta}.

(A.17)

Under
A.1
, for
|t-s|\leq h
,

\omega_{A}(h)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}s,t\in[0,T]\\
|t-s|\leq h\end{subarray}}\|A_{t}-A_{s}\|\leq\left|\int_{s}^{t}\|b_{u}^{\beta}\|du\right|\leq Kh.

(A.18)

Moreover,
M
is a continuous local martingale, which is a time-changed Brownian motion by the Dambis-Dubins-Schwarz theorem. By Lévy’s modulus of continuity for Brownian motion, it holds that when
h\to 0
,

\omega_{M}(h)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}s,t\in[0,T]\\
|t-s|\leq h\end{subarray}}\|M_{t}-M_{s}\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{h\log\frac{1}{h}}\right).

(A.19)

Therefore, we obtain

\omega_{\beta^{c}}(h)\leq\omega_{A}(h)+\omega_{M}(h)=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{h\log\frac{1}{h}}\right).

(A.20)

Taking
h=(d+1)H_{n}\asymp K_{n}^{-1}
, by
Eq.

A.16
, it holds that

\|\beta^{c}-\overline{\beta}^{c}\|_{L^{2}}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log K_{n}}{K_{n}}}\right).

(A.21)

Next, we verify that
\|\beta^{j}-\overline{\beta}^{j}\|_{L^{2}}=O_{p}(K_{n}^{-1/2})
. Define
V_{\beta^{j}}([a,b])
the total variation of
\beta^{j}
on the interval
[a,b]
, i.e.,

V_{\beta^{j}}([a,b])=\int_{a}^{b}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\|\delta^{\beta}(s,x)\|\mu^{\beta}(ds,dx).

(A.22)

Then for any
s,t\in[a,b]
,
\|\beta_{t}^{j}-\beta_{s}^{j}\|\leq V_{\beta^{j}}([a,b])
. Therefore, whenever
B_{t}^{(k)}>0
,
\|\beta_{t}^{j}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}^{j}\|\leq V_{\beta^{j}}(\text{supp}(B^{(k)}))
. By the convexity of the norm,
\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}B_{t}^{(k)}=1
, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\|\beta_{t}^{j}-\overline{\beta}_{t}^{j}\|\leq\sum_{k\in\{k:B_{t}^{(k)}>0\}}\|\beta_{t}^{j}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}^{j}\|B_{t}^{(k)}\leq\sum_{k\in\{k:B_{t}^{(k)}>0\}}B_{t}^{(k)}V_{\beta^{j}}(\text{supp}(B^{(k)})).

(A.23)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
|\{k:B_{t}^{(k)}>0\}|\leq d+1
,

\|\beta_{t}^{j}-\overline{\beta}_{t}^{j}\|^{2}\leq(d+1)\sum_{k\in\{k:B_{t}^{(k)}>0\}}V_{\beta^{j}}^{2}(\text{supp}(B^{(k)})).

(A.24)

Therefore, we have

\begin{split}\|\beta^{j}-\overline{\beta}^{j}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}&=\int_{0}^{T}\|\beta_{t}^{j}-\overline{\beta}_{t}^{j}\|^{2}dt\leq(d+1)\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}V_{\beta^{j}}^{2}(\text{supp}(B^{(k)}))\int_{0}^{T}\mathbbm{1}_{\{B_{t}^{(k)}>0\}}dt\\
&\leq(d+1)|\text{supp}(B^{(k)})|\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}V_{\beta^{j}}^{2}(\text{supp}(B^{(k)}))\leq(d+1)^{2}H_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}V_{\beta^{j}}^{2}(\text{supp}(B^{(k)})).\end{split}

(A.25)

Since supp(
B^{(k)}
) covers at most
d+1
knot intervals and each knot interval belongs to the supports of at most
d+1
B-splines,

\begin{split}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}V_{\beta^{j}}^{2}(\text{supp}(B^{(k)}))&\leq(d+1)^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{n}+1}V_{\beta^{j}}^{2}([v_{j-1},v_{j}])\\
&\leq(d+1)^{2}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N_{n}+1}V_{\beta^{j}}([v_{j-1},v_{j}])\right)^{2}\leq(d+1)^{2}V_{\beta^{j}}^{2}([0,T]).\end{split}

(A.26)

Therefore, under
2
,
V_{\beta^{j}}([0,T])=O_{p}(1)
, and

\|\beta^{j}-\overline{\beta}^{j}\|_{L^{2}}\leq K\sqrt{H_{n}}V_{\beta^{j}}([0,T])=O_{p}(K_{n}^{-1/2}).

(A.27)

Combining
Eqs.

A.13
,
A.21
and
A.27
leads to
Eq.

A.10
. This completes the proof.

A.3
Proof of
Theorem

1

Identifiability

We start with the identifiability of
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}
by verifying that
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}
is nonsingular. We first prove that the theoretical Gram matrix is positive definite, and then show that the theoretical and empirical Gram matrices are uniformly close over the estimation space, such that the empirical identifiability holds with
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}
uniquely defined; see Section 2 in
Huang, 2003
for further details.

Define the matrix
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}
as the theoretical counterpart of
\mathbf{R}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\int_{0}^{\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(1)}\,dX_{1,s}^{c}&\cdots&\int_{0}^{\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(K_{n})}\,dX_{1,s}^{c}&\cdots&\int_{0}^{\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(1)}\,dX_{p,s}^{c}&\cdots&\int_{0}^{\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(K_{n})}\,dX_{p,s}^{c}\\[4.30554pt]
\int_{\Delta_{n}}^{2\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(1)}\,dX_{1,s}^{c}&\cdots&\int_{\Delta_{n}}^{2\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(K_{n})}\,dX_{1,s}^{c}&\cdots&\int_{\Delta_{n}}^{2\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(1)}\,dX_{p,s}^{c}&\cdots&\int_{\Delta_{n}}^{2\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(K_{n})}\,dX_{p,s}^{c}\\[4.30554pt]
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\[4.30554pt]
\int_{(n-1)\Delta_{n}}^{n\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(1)}\,dX_{1,s}^{c}&\cdots&\int_{(n-1)\Delta_{n}}^{n\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(K_{n})}\,dX_{1,s}^{c}&\cdots&\int_{(n-1)\Delta_{n}}^{n\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(1)}\,dX_{p,s}^{c}&\cdots&\int_{(n-1)\Delta_{n}}^{n\Delta_{n}}B_{s}^{(K_{n})}\,dX_{p,s}^{c}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\mathbf{B}_{s}^{\top}dX^{c}_{s}\right)_{1\leq i\leq n}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times pK_{n}},

(A.28)

and the corresponding Gram matrix is
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}
. Let
g_{t}=(g_{1,t},\dots,g_{p,t})^{\top}
with
g_{j,t}=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}B_{t}^{(k)}v_{j}^{(k)}
for any
v_{j}^{(k)}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\bm{v}=(v_{1}^{(1)},\dots,v_{1}^{(K_{n})},\dots,v_{p}^{(1)},\dots,v_{p}^{(K_{n})})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{pK_{n}}
, and
g\in\mathbb{G}_{n}
by construction. By the properties of B-splines (Appendix
A.1
(iv)) as well as bounded and nonsingular
c
, as
\Delta_{n}\to 0
,

\bm{v}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\bm{v}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\int_{0}^{T}g_{s}^{\top}c_{s}g_{s}ds\asymp\sum_{j=1}^{p}\|g_{j}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\asymp\frac{\|\bm{v}\|^{2}}{K_{n}},\qquad\text{where }\|\bm{v}\|^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}(v_{j}^{(k)})^{2},

(A.29)

which implies that
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}
is positive definite. The standard law of large numbers (LLN) result for truncated functionals (Theorem 9.2.1,
Jacod and Protter, 2012
) implies that, with
u_{n}\asymp\Delta_{n}^{\varpi}
for some
0<\varpi<1/2
,

\bm{v}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\bm{v}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\int_{0}^{T}g_{s}^{\top}c_{s}g_{s}ds,

(A.30)

as
\Delta_{n}\to 0
. By Lemma A.2 in
Huang et al., 2004
, it holds that

\sup_{g\in\mathbb{G}_{n}}\left|\frac{\bm{v}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\bm{v}}{\bm{v}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\bm{v}}-1\right|=o_{p}(1),

(A.31)

if
\Delta_{n}K_{n}\log K_{n}\to 0
. Therefore, the empirical Gram matrix
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}
is nonsingular and
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}
is uniquely defined with probability approaching one.

By
Eqs.

A.29
and
A.31
, for some
\epsilon_{n}=o_{p}(1)
and
0\leq\epsilon_{n}<1
with probability approaching one,

\bm{v}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\bm{v}\geq(1-\epsilon_{n})\bm{v}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\bm{v}\asymp(1-\epsilon_{n})\frac{\|\bm{v}\|^{2}}{K_{n}}.

(A.32)

Let
\|\bm{v}\|\asymp 1
, the eigenvalue bound of Rayleigh quotient implies that
\lambda_{\text{min}}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})\geq K/K_{n}
, and thus
\|(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\|=(\lambda_{\text{min}}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}))^{-1}=O_{p}(K_{n})
. A similar but reverse inequality to
Eq.

A.32
indicates that
\lambda_{\text{max}}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})=O_{p}(K_{n}^{-1})
and
\|\mathbf{R}\|=\sqrt{\lambda_{\text{max}}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})}=O_{p}(K_{n}^{-1/2})
. The probabilistic bounds for
\|(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\|
and
\|\mathbf{R}\|
will be used in the following proofs.

Consistency of
\widehat{\beta}

The truncated increment of
Y
in
Eq.

1
over the
i
-th interval is

\begin{split}\mathbf{Y}_{i}&=\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\beta_{s-}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}+\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}+\Delta_{i}^{n}Z\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
&=\underbrace{\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\widetilde{\beta}_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}}+\underbrace{\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{\mathbf{U}_{i}}\\
&\qquad+\underbrace{\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{\mathbf{J}_{i}}+\underbrace{\Delta_{i}^{n}Z\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}},\end{split}

(A.33)

where
\widetilde{\beta}=\mathbf{B}\bm{\gamma}
is the spline approximation of
\beta
, and
e_{t}=\beta_{t-}-\widetilde{\beta}_{t}
.

Remark A.1

(Predictability)
.

Note that, since
H=\widetilde{\beta}
or
e
is not predictable, whenever we write the stochastic integral
\int H_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}
, it is understood as
\int(^{p}H_{s})^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}
, where
{}^{p}H
is the predictable projection with
{}^{p}H_{\tau}=\mathbb{E}[H_{\tau}|\mathcal{F}_{\tau-}]
for all predictable times
\tau
; see Theorem I.2.28 in
Jacod and Shiryaev, 2013
.

Since
\beta_{\tau-}
is predictable under
1
(i), by Proposition I.2.18 in
Jacod and Shiryaev, 2013
and linearity of predictable projection, it holds that
{}^{p}\beta_{\tau-}=\,^{p}\widetilde{\beta}_{\tau}+\,^{p}e_{\tau}=\beta_{\tau-}
. In particular, we will apply the Itô isometry and the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy (BDG) inequality to
{}^{p}H
. Moreover, by Jensen’s inequality,
\|^{p}H_{s}\|^{2}\leq\,^{p}(\|H_{s}\|^{2})
, and thus whenever we use Itô isometry,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}H_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}(^{p}H_{s})^{\top}c_{s}(^{p}H_{s})ds\right]\leq K\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\|H_{s}\|^{2}ds\right]=K\mathbb{E}[\|H\|_{L^{2}}^{2}].

(A.34)

by the boundedness of
c
under
A.1
.

Then, by
Eq.

18
,

\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}-\bm{\gamma}=(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}((\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma})+\mathbf{U}+\mathbf{J}+\mathbf{Z}).

(A.35)

To prove
\|\widehat{\beta}-\beta\|_{L^{2}}=o_{p}(1)
, we verify that the following four components are all
o_{p}(1)
:

\begin{split}\|\widehat{\beta}-\beta\|_{L^{2}}&\leq\|\widehat{\beta}-\widetilde{\beta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|e\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq\,\underbrace{\|\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma})\|_{L^{2}}}_{A_{1}}\,+\,\underbrace{\|\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}}}_{A_{2}}\,\\
&\qquad+\,\underbrace{\|\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{J}\|_{L^{2}}}_{A_{3}}\,+\,\underbrace{\|\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\|_{L^{2}}}_{A_{4}}\,+\,\|e\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

(A.36)

where
\|e\|_{L^{2}}=o_{p}(1)
by
Lemma

1
.

We start with two lemmas.
Lemma

A.1
is a result from the properties of B-spline basis functions and will be used to derive probabilistic bounds for the components listed above.
Lemma

A.2
provides some results regarding the truncation technique of
Mancini, 2009
, and will be used repeatedly in subsequent proofs.

Lemma A.1
.

For any vector
\bm{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
\|\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\bm{r}\|_{L^{2}}\leq K\sqrt{K_{n}}\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}\bm{r}\|\leq K^{\prime}\|\bm{r}\|
.

Proof of
Lemma

A.1
.

It holds that, by Appendix
A.1
(iv) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\bm{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}&\asymp K_{n}^{-1}\|(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\bm{r}\|^{2}\leq KK_{n}^{-1}\|(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\|^{2}\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}\bm{r}\|^{2}\\
&\leq K^{\prime}K_{n}\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}\|\bm{r}\|^{2}\leq K^{\prime\prime}\|\bm{r}\|^{2},\end{split}

(A.37)

where
\|(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\|=O_{p}(K_{n})
and
\|\mathbf{R}\|=O_{p}(K_{n}^{-1/2})
from
Eq.

A.32
.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Let
u_{n}\asymp\Delta_{n}^{\varpi}
for some
0<\varpi<1/2
. Then, as
\Delta_{n}\to 0
,

(i)

\mathbb{P}(\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|>u_{n})\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}
,
\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}
;

(ii)

\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}=O_{p}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r})
.

Proof of
Lemma

A.2
.

(i) Under
1
(v), it holds that for
0<u<1
,

\int_{\{\|\delta\|>u\}}\lambda(dx)\leq u^{-r}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|^{r}\wedge 1)\lambda(dx)\leq u^{-r}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}f_{m}(x)\lambda(dx)\leq Ku^{-r},

(A.38)

\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq u\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|^{2}\lambda(dx)\leq u^{2-r}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|^{r}\wedge 1)\lambda(dx)\leq Ku^{2-r}.

(A.39)

\begin{split}\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq u\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\lambda(dx)&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\delta(s,x)\|\leq u\}}\lambda(dx)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left(\int_{0}^{\|\delta(s,x)\|}\mathbbm{1}_{\{y\leq u\}}dy\right)\lambda(dx)\\
&=\int_{0}^{u}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\delta(s,x)\|>y\}}\lambda(dx)\right)dy-u\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\delta(s,x)\|>u\}}\lambda(dx)\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{u}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\delta(s,x)\|>y\}}\lambda(dx)\right)dy\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{u}y^{-r}\,dy\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|^{r}\wedge 1)\lambda(dx)\\
&\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}f_{m}(x)\lambda(dx)\right)\int_{0}^{u}y^{-r}dy\leq Ku^{1-r}.\end{split}

(A.40)

Define the count of large jumps and the sum of small jumps with the cutoff level
u_{n}
:

\displaystyle N_{i}^{n}(u_{n})

\displaystyle=\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\delta(s,x)\|>u_{n}\}}\mu(ds,dx),

(A.41)

\displaystyle S_{i}^{n}(u_{n})

\displaystyle=\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq u_{n}\}}\delta(s,x)\mu(ds,dx),

(A.42)

and consider the following event decomposition:

E_{n}=\left\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq\frac{u_{n}}{2},\,N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)=0\right\},\qquad E_{n}^{\complement}=\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|>u_{n}\}\backslash E_{n}.

(A.43)

By the triangle inequality, on
E_{n}
,

\left\|S_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\right\|=\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\geq\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|-\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>\frac{u_{n}}{2},

(A.44)

so that
\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|>u_{n}\}\subseteq\{\|S_{i}^{n}(u_{n}/2)\|>u_{n}/2\}\cup E_{n}^{\complement}
. Therefore,

\mathbb{P}(\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|>u_{n})\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>\frac{u_{n}}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\geq 1\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|S_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\right\|>\frac{u_{n}}{2}\right),

(A.45)

so it suffices to bound all three probabilities on the right-hand side in
Eq.

A.45
.

Choose any
q\geq 2
that satisfies
q>\frac{1-\varpi r}{1/2-\varpi}
, and it holds that

\mathbb{P}\left(\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>\frac{u_{n}}{2}\right)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{q}]}{(u_{n}/2)^{q}}\leq K\Delta_{n}^{q/2}u_{n}^{-q}=o(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}).

(A.46)

by Markov’s inequality and the BDG inequality. Then, by
Eq.

A.38
,

\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\geq 1\right)\leq\mathbb{E}\left[N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}ds\int_{\{\|\delta\|>u_{n}/2\}}\lambda(dx)\right]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}.

(A.47)

By subadditivity of the Euclidean norm,

\left\|S_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\right\|\leq\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq u_{n}/2\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx),

(A.48)

and Markov’s inequality implies that

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|S_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\right\|>\frac{u_{n}}{2}\right)&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq u_{n}/2\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)>\frac{u_{n}}{2}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{2}{u_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}ds\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq u_{n}/2\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\lambda(dx)\right]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r},\end{split}

(A.49)

by
Eq.

A.40
. Combining
Eqs.

A.46
,
A.47
and
A.49
in
Eq.

A.45
, we obtain

\mathbb{P}(\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|>u_{n})\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}.

(A.50)

We write
\Delta_{i}^{n}Y
into

\Delta_{i}^{n}Y=\,\underbrace{\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\beta_{s-}^{\top}b_{s}ds+\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\beta_{s-}^{\top}\sigma_{s}dW_{s}}_{\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}}\,\,+\,\,\underbrace{\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}}_{\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(2)}}\,\,+\,\,\Delta_{i}^{n}Z,

(A.51)

and by the triangle inequality,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>\frac{u_{n}}{4}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(2)}|>\frac{u_{n}}{4}\right)\\
&\qquad+\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|>\frac{u_{n}}{4}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|>\frac{u_{n}}{4}\right).\end{split}

(A.52)

By bounded
\beta
under
A.1
and the BDG inequality, for any
q>2
,

\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{q}]\leq K\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Delta_{n}^{2}+\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\|\beta_{s}^{\top}\sigma_{s}\|^{2}ds\right)^{q/2}\right]\leq K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}^{q/2}.

(A.53)

Then by Markov’s inequality,

\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>\frac{u_{n}}{4}\right)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{q}]}{(u_{n}/4)^{q}}\leq K\Delta_{n}^{q/2}u_{n}^{-q}=o(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}),\qquad\text{if }q>\frac{1-\varpi r}{1/2-\varpi},

(A.54)

which is always possible because
0<\varpi<1/2
and
0\leq r<1
.

By bounded
\beta^{J}
under
A.1
, there exists some deterministic
M>0
such that

|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(2)}|\leq\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}\|\beta_{s}^{J}\|\|\Delta X_{s}\|\leq M\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}\|\Delta X_{s}\|,

(A.55)

and thus

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(2)}|>\frac{u_{n}}{4}\right)&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{8M}\Bigr)\geq 1\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq u_{n}/8M\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)>\frac{u_{n}}{8M}\right)\\
&\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r},\end{split}

(A.56)

which follows the same steps as in
Eq.

A.47
and the second inequality in
Eq.

A.49
.

For the last two terms in
Eq.

A.52
, we apply the same arguments to the one-dimensional Itô semimartingale
Z
under
1
(vi). Combining them,
Eqs.

A.54
and
A.56
in
Eq.

A.52
leads to

\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}.

(A.57)

This completes the proof of
Lemma

A.2
(i).

(ii) We consider the following event decomposition:

\begin{split}\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}=\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n},\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq u_{n}\}\cup\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n},\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}.\end{split}

(A.58)

For each
i
, on the event
\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n},\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq u_{n}\}
,

\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|+\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq 2u_{n},

(A.59)

and thus

\begin{split}\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}&\leq\underbrace{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq 2u_{n}}}_{V_{i}^{(1)}}\\
&\qquad+\underbrace{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}}\mathbbm{1}_{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}}}_{V_{i}^{(2)}}.\end{split}

(A.60)

For
V_{i}^{(1)}
, since the function
x\mapsto x\wedge a
is subadditive,

V_{i}^{(1)}\leq(\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\wedge 2u_{n})^{2}\leq\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}.

(A.61)

Hence, using
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
and
\mu=\nu+(\mu-\nu)
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[V_{i}^{(1)}]&\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})(\mu-\nu)(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\qquad+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\nu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right].\end{split}

(A.62)

For the first term in
Eq.

A.62
, by the Itô isometry for compensated Poisson integrals,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})(\mu-\nu)(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\qquad=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})^{2}\nu(ds,dx)\right]\\
&\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}ds\left(\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq 2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|^{2}\lambda(dx)+4u_{n}^{2}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\lambda(dx)\right)\right]\\
&\qquad\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r},\end{split}

(A.63)

by
Eqs.

A.39
and
A.38
. For the second term in
Eq.

A.62
, by Jensen’s inequality,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\nu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\qquad\leq\Delta_{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\lambda(dx)\right)^{2}\right]ds\\
&\qquad\leq\Delta_{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq 2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\lambda(dx)+2u_{n}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\lambda(dx)\right)^{2}\right]ds\\
&\qquad\leq K\Delta_{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}u_{n}^{2-2r}ds=K\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{2-2r},\end{split}

(A.64)

by
Eqs.

A.38
and
A.40
. Combining
Eqs.

A.62
,
A.63
and
A.64
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}[V_{i}^{(1)}]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}+K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{2-2r}\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}.

(A.65)

For
V_{i}^{(2)}
, on the event
\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n},\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}
,

\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq u_{n}+\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq 2\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|,

(A.66)

and thus
\mathbb{E}[V_{i}^{(2)}]\leq 4\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}}\right]
. It holds that, for any
q>2
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\left(\frac{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|}{u_{n}}\right)^{q-2}\right]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{q}]}{u_{n}^{q-2}}\leq\Delta_{n}^{q/2}u_{n}^{2-q}.\end{split}

(A.67)

by the BDG inequality. Therefore,

\mathbb{E}[V_{i}^{(2)}]\leq\Delta_{n}^{q/2}u_{n}^{2-q}=o(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}),\qquad\text{if }q>\frac{1-\varpi r}{1/2-\varpi},

(A.68)

which is always possible because
0<\varpi<1/2
and
0\leq r<1
.

Finally, by
Eqs.

A.60
,
A.65
and
A.68
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]=O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}),

(A.69)

and thus, by Markov’s inequality,

\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}=O_{p}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}).

(A.70)

This completes the proof of
Lemma

A.2
(ii).

∎

Next, we verify that
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
,
A_{3}
and
A_{4}
in
Eq.

A.36
are all
o_{p}(1)
:

(i)
\bm{A_{1}=o_{p}(1)}

We consider a further decomposition
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(1)}+\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(2)}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}},

(A.71)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}.

(A.72)

The
i
-th element in
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma}
is given by

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\Delta_{i}^{n}X\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
=\,\,&\underbrace{\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right)}_{M_{i}^{(1)}}\,-\,\underbrace{\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{M_{i}^{(2)}},\end{split}

(A.73)

and thus

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(M_{i}^{(1)}-M_{i}^{(2)})^{2}\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{n}(M_{i}^{(1)})^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{n}(M_{i}^{(2)})^{2}.

(A.74)

For
M_{i}^{(1)}
and
M_{i}^{(2)}
, since
\|\widetilde{\beta}\|_{\infty}
is bounded by de Boor’s conjecture (see, e.g.,
de Boor, 1973
;
Shadrin, 2001
), it holds that, by the BDG inequality and
Lemma

A.2
(i),

\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[(M_{i}^{(1)})^{2}]&\leq K\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right)\right]\\
&\leq K\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right)\right]\\
&\leq K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})+\mathbb{P}(\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|>u_{n}))\\
&\leq K^{\prime\prime}\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}=o(1),\end{split}

(A.75)

and, by
Lemma

A.2
(ii),

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[(M_{i}^{(2)})^{2}]\leq K\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\leq Ku_{n}^{2-r}=o(1).

(A.76)

Therefore,
\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma}\|^{2}]=o(1)
, and thus
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma}\|=o_{p}(1)
by Markov’s inequality.

Next, we verify that
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(2)}\|=o_{p}(1)
.
By the Itô isometry (see
Remark

A.1
) and bounded
c
,

\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(2)}\|^{2}]\leq K\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\|^{2}]ds.

(A.77)

Since
\widetilde{\beta}
is absolutely continuous and has bounded derivative on
[0,T]
,

\|\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\|\leq(s-(i-1)\Delta_{n})\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{\infty},\qquad\text{where }\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}_{s}\|,

(A.78)

and
\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}_{s}
stands for the derivative of
\widetilde{\beta}
on
s
. By
Eqs.

A.77
and
A.78
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(2)}\|^{2}]&\leq K\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{2}]\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(s-(i-1)\Delta_{n})^{2}ds\\
&\leq K\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{2}]\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}^{2}ds=K\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{2}]\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{3}\\
&\leq K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{2}]=O(\Delta_{n}^{2}K_{n}^{2})=o(1).\end{split}

(A.79)

because
\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(K_{n})
and
\Delta_{n}K_{n}\log K_{n}\to 0
, and thus
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(2)}\|=o_{p}(1)
by Markov’s inequality.

By
Lemma

A.1
, it holds that

\begin{split}A_{1}&\leq\|\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma})\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq K\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma}\|+K^{\prime}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(2)}\|=o_{p}(1).\end{split}

(A.80)

(ii)
\bm{A_{2}=o_{p}(1)}

Conditional on a
\sigma
-field
\mathcal{G}=\sigma(e_{s}:0\leq s\leq T)
, it holds that
\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{U}\|^{2}|\mathcal{G}]\leq K\|e\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
by the Itô isometry and bounded
c
. Fix
\varepsilon>0
. For any
\eta>0
, it holds that by Chebyshev’s inequality,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{U}\|>\varepsilon)&\leq\mathbb{P}(\|e\|_{L^{2}}>\eta)+\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{U}\|>\varepsilon,\|e\|_{L^{2}}\leq\eta)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(\|e\|_{L^{2}}>\eta)+\frac{\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{U}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|e\|_{L^{2}}\leq\eta\}}]}{\varepsilon^{2}}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(\|e\|_{L^{2}}>\eta)+\frac{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{U}\|^{2}|\mathcal{G}]\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|e\|_{L^{2}}\leq\eta\}}]}{\varepsilon^{2}}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(\|e\|_{L^{2}}>\eta)+K\frac{\mathbb{E}[\|e\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|e\|_{L^{2}}\leq\eta\}}]}{\varepsilon^{2}}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(\|e\|_{L^{2}}>\eta)+K\frac{\eta^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}},\end{split}

(A.81)

where
\mathbb{P}(\|e\|_{L^{2}}>\eta)\to 0
since
\|e\|_{L^{2}}=o_{p}(1)
by
Lemma

1
. Let
\eta\downarrow 0
, and then
\|\mathbf{U}\|=o_{p}(1)
. Therefore,
A_{2}\leq K\|\mathbf{U}\|=o_{p}(1)
by
Lemma

A.1
.

(iii)
\bm{A_{3}=o_{p}(1)}

We define a vector
\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with the
i
-th element

\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}=\mathbf{J}_{i}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}=\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}.

(A.82)

Because the design matrix
\mathbf{R}
already includes the same truncation
\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}
for covariate increments, we have
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{J}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}
. Hence, by
Lemma

A.1
, it holds that

A_{3}\leq K\sqrt{K_{n}}\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{J}\|=K\sqrt{K_{n}}\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}\|\leq K^{\prime}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}\|,

(A.83)

so that it suffices to show that
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}\|=o_{p}(1)
. By bounded
\beta^{J}
under
A.1
, it holds that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}\|\beta_{s-}^{J}\|\|\Delta X_{s}\|\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\leq K\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}\|\Delta X_{s}\|\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}

\displaystyle\leq K\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}

\displaystyle\leq K\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx)+\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}

\displaystyle\leq 2K\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2K\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}},

(A.84)

and thus

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}]&\leq K\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\qquad+K^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right].\end{split}

(A.85)

Following the same steps as in
Eqs.

A.62
,
A.63
,
A.64
and
A.65
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}.

(A.86)

With bounded
\delta
under
A.1
, it holds that

\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\leq K(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}},

(A.87)

where
N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})
is defined in
Eq.

A.41
. Therefore, the left-hand side in the above inequality must be zero unless on the event
\{N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})\geq 1,\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}
. We consider the following event relations:

\begin{split}\{N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})\geq 1,\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}&\subseteq\left\{N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})\geq 1,\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n},\,N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)=1\right\}\\
&\qquad\cup\left\{N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})\geq 1,\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n},\,N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\geq 2\right\}\\
&\subseteq\left\{N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})=1,\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\right\}\cup\left\{N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\geq 2\right\}\end{split}

(A.88)

On the event
\{N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})=1,\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}
, by the triangle inequality,

\left\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}+S_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\right\|>2u_{n}-\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|>u_{n},

(A.89)

and therefore

\{N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})\geq 1,\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}\,\subseteq\,\underbrace{\left\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>\frac{u_{n}}{2}\right\}}_{E_{n}^{(1)}}\,\cup\,\underbrace{\left\{\left\|S_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\right\|>\frac{u_{n}}{2}\right\}}_{E_{n}^{(2)}}\,\cup\,\underbrace{\left\{N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\geq 2\right\}}_{E_{n}^{(3)}}.

(A.90)

Then, by
Eq.

A.87
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\qquad\leq K\mathbb{E}\left[(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}^{(1)}}\right]+K^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}^{(2)}}\right]+K^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}^{(3)}}\right].\end{split}

(A.91)

By the factorial moment of Poisson random variable and
Eq.

A.38
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}^{(3)}}\right]&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}^{(3)}}\right]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}\Bigr)\right)\left(N_{i}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{u_{n}}{2}-1\Bigr)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}^{(3)}}\right]\\
&\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}ds\int_{\{\|\delta\|>u_{n}/2\}}\lambda(dx)\right)^{2}\right]\leq K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r}.\end{split}

(A.92)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
Eqs.

A.38
and
A.47
, take some
q\geq 2
and
q>\frac{6(1-\varpi r)}{1-2\varpi}
, then

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}^{(1)}}\right]&\leq\left(\mathbb{E}[(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>\frac{u_{n}}{2}\right)\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq K(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r})^{1/2}(\Delta_{n}^{q/2}u_{n}^{-q})^{1/2}=K\Delta_{n}^{1/2+q/4}u_{n}^{-(r+q)/2}=o(\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r}).\end{split}

(A.93)

Following a similar approach to
Eq.

A.49
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}^{(2)}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{2}\left(\frac{2}{u_{n}}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq u_{n}/2\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{u_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})-1)\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq u_{n}/2\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2}{u_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq u_{n}/2\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{u_{n}}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r})^{2}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r})+\frac{K^{\prime}}{u_{n}}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r})(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r})\leq K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r}.

(A.94)

by
Eqs.

A.38
and
A.40
, and the fact that
\{\|\delta(s,x)\|>2u_{n}\}
and
\{\|\delta(s,x)\|\leq u_{n}/2\}
are disjoint.

Combining
Eqs.

A.93
,
A.94
and
A.92
in
Eq.

A.91
leads to

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\leq K\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r}.

(A.95)

Then, combining
Eqs.

A.86
and
A.95
in
Eq.

A.85
, we have

\mathbb{E}[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}+K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r},

(A.96)

and therefore

\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}\|^{2}]\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}]\leq Ku_{n}^{2-r}+K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-2r}=o(1).

(A.97)

Therefore, we have
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}\|=o_{p}(1)
by Markov’s inequality and thus
A_{3}=o_{p}(1)
.

(iv)
\bm{A_{4}=o_{p}(1)}

We denote the
i^{\prime}
-th element in
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{pK_{n}}
for
i^{\prime}(j,k)=(j-1)K_{n}+k
by

C_{j}^{(k)}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(k)}\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X\Delta_{i}^{n}Z\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}.

(A.98)

By Appendix
A.1
(iv),

\Delta_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(k)})^{2}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\int_{0}^{T}(B_{s}^{(k)})^{2}ds\asymp K_{n}^{-1}.

(A.99)

Under the orthogonality condition in
3
,
X
and
Z
have no co-jumps, and the (fixed-weighted) realized covariance
\sum_{i=1}^{n}B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(k)}\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X\Delta_{i}^{n}Z
has zero limit. By the standard results of realized covariance functionals (see, e.g., Theorem 10.3.2,
Jacod and Protter, 2012
), it holds that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(k)}\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X\Delta_{i}^{n}Z\right)^{2}\right]=O(K_{n}^{-1}\Delta_{n}),

(A.100)

and thus
\mathbb{E}[(C_{j}^{(k)})^{2}]=O(K_{n}^{-1}\Delta_{n})
. Therefore,

\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\|^{2}]=\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}\mathbb{E}[(C_{j}^{(k)})^{2}]=O(\Delta_{n}),

(A.101)

and thus
\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\|=O_{p}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}})
. By
Lemma

A.1
,

A_{4}\leq K\sqrt{K_{n}}\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\|=O_{p}(\sqrt{K_{n}\Delta_{n}})=o_{p}(1),

(A.102)

when
\Delta_{n}K_{n}\log K_{n}\to 0
.

Therefore, all components in
Eq.

A.36
are
o_{p}(1)
, and thus
\|\widehat{\beta}-\beta\|_{L^{2}}=o_{p}(1)
. This completes the proof.

A.4
Proof of
Theorem

2

We start with the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}(\widehat{I\beta}_{T}-I\beta_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}-\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}\bm{\gamma}\,ds-\int_{0}^{T}e_{s}ds\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\left(\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}\Delta_{n}-\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}\bm{\gamma}ds\right)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\int_{0}^{T}e_{s}ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}-\bm{\gamma})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds-\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)\bm{\gamma}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\int_{0}^{T}e_{s}ds.

(A.103)

To derive the asymptotic distribution of the
p
-dimensional vector in the first term by the Cramér-Wold device, we need to show that, for any fixed linear combination with
a\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,

\begin{split}H_{n}&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\,a^{\top}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}-\bm{\gamma})\\
&\qquad\xrightarrow{\mathcal{L}-s}\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}^{2}}\left(a^{\top}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds\right)\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}^{\top}c_{s}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds\right)^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{s}^{\top}\right)^{\top}c_{s}^{1/2}dW^{\prime}_{s},\end{split}

(A.104)

where
W^{\prime}
is a standard
p
-dimensional Brownian motion defined on an extension of the original probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0},\mathbb{P})
. We also show that the second term (the bias induced by the discretization error) and the third term (the bias induced by the spline approximation error) in
Eq.

A.103
are both asymptotically negligible as
\Delta_{n}\to 0
.

For the fixed linear combination in
Eq.

A.104
, we consider a further decomposition by
Eq.

A.35
:

\displaystyle H_{n}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\,a^{\top}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma})}_{H_{1,n}}\,+\,\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\,a^{\top}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{U}}_{H_{2,n}}

\displaystyle+\,\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\,a^{\top}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{J}}_{H_{3,n}}\,+\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\,a^{\top}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{Z},

(A.105)

where
\mathbf{Z}=(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{Z}_{n})^{\top}
in the fourth term includes the truncation
\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}
on each increment of
Y
rather than on increments of
Z
(see
Eq.

A.33
). Therefore, we rewrite the fourth term into:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\,a^{\top}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\,a^{\top}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}}_{H_{4,n}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\,a^{\top}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\mathbf{Z}-\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}})}_{H_{5,n}},

(A.106)

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}=(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})^{\top}
with
\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}=\Delta_{i}^{n}Z\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq u_{n}\}}
. Next, we verify that the leading term for the stable central limit theorem (CLT) in
Eq.

A.104
is
H_{4,n}
, and the other components
H_{1,n}
,
H_{2,n}
,
H_{3,n}
, and
H_{5,n}
are asymptotically negligible.

(i) Asymptotic distribution of
\bm{H_{4,n}}

We define the following vectors:

\bm{w}_{n}=(w_{1,n}^{(1)},\dots,w_{1,n}^{(K_{n})},\dots,w_{p,n}^{(1)},\dots,w_{p,n}^{(K_{n})})^{\top}=(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)^{\top}a\in\mathbb{R}^{pK_{n}},

(A.107)

\theta_{n,t}=\mathbf{B}_{t}\bm{w}_{n}=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}B_{t}^{(k)}\begin{pmatrix}w_{1,n}^{(k)}\\
\vdots\\
w_{p,n}^{(k)}\\
\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}.

(A.108)

By the LLN result in
Eq.

A.30
, a standard Riemann approximation for the deterministic integral, and the continuous mapping theorem,
\theta_{n,t}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\theta_{t}=\mathbf{B}_{t}\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
for any
0\leq t\leq T
, where

\bm{w}=(w_{1}^{(1)},\dots,w_{p}^{(K_{n})})^{\top}=\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}^{\top}c_{s}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds\right)^{-1}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds\right)^{\top}a\in\mathbb{R}^{pK_{n}}.

(A.109)

By definition,

H_{4,n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\Delta_{i}^{n}X\Delta_{i}^{n}Z\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq u_{n}\}}.

(A.110)

Under
3
,
X
and
Z
have zero continuous covariation and no co-jumps. By the standard CLT for truncated realized covariances (Theorem 13.2.1,
Jacod and Protter, 2012
), and stable Slutsky’s theorem, it holds that

H_{4,n}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{L}-s}\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}^{2}}\theta_{s}^{\top}c_{s}^{1/2}dW^{\prime}_{s},

(A.111)

where
W^{\prime}
is a standard
p
-dimensional Brownian motion defined on an extension of the original probability space. The conditional variance of the limit equals

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}^{2}\theta_{s}^{\top}c_{s}\theta_{s}ds&=a^{\top}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds\right)\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}^{\top}c_{s}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds\right)^{-1}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}^{2}\mathbf{B}_{s}^{\top}c_{s}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds\right)\\
&\qquad\times\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}^{\top}c_{s}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds\right)^{-1}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds\right)^{\top}a\\
&=a^{\top}\Sigma_{T}^{\beta}a.\end{split}

(A.112)

(ii)
\bm{H_{1,n}=o_{p}(1)}

Decompose
H_{1,n}
into:

\begin{split}H_{1,n}&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{w}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i}\mathcal{Y}_{i}\\
&\leq\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{w}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i}M_{i}^{(1)}}_{H_{1,n}^{(1)}}\,\,-\,\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{w}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i}M_{i}^{(2)}}_{H_{1,n}^{(2)}}\,\,+\,\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{w}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{(2)}}_{H_{1,n}^{(3)}},\end{split}

(A.113)

where
\mathcal{Y}_{i}
represents the
i
-th element of
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma}
, and we employ the decomposition of
\mathcal{Y}_{i}
in
Eqs.

A.71
,
A.72
and
A.73
:

\mathcal{Y}_{i}=M_{i}^{(1)}-M_{i}^{(2)}+\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{(2)},

(A.114)

Next, we verify that all three components on the right-hand side of
Eq.

A.113
are
o_{p}(1)
.

(ii.1)
\bm{H_{1,n}^{(1)}=o_{p}(1)}

It holds for
H_{1,n}^{(1)}
that, by Hölder’s inequality,

|H_{1,n}^{(1)}|=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{w}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i}M_{i}^{(1)}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\bm{w}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i}||M_{i}^{(1)}|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\|\mathbf{R}_{i}\|_{1}|M_{i}^{(1)}|.

(A.115)

For any
i=1,\dots,n
, by the local support of B-splines (Appendix
A.1
(iii)), each
\mathbf{R}_{i}
has only a fixed number of nonzero components, and then the equivalence between
\ell_{1}
- and
\ell_{2}
-norms implies that

|H_{1,n}^{(1)}|\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathbf{R}_{i}\||M_{i}^{(1)}|.

(A.116)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\begin{split}|H_{1,n}^{(1)}|&\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\|\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\||M_{i}^{(1)}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
&\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\left(K^{\prime}\max_{1\leq k\leq K_{n}}B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(k)}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\||M_{i}^{(1)}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
&\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\||M_{i}^{(1)}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
&\leq\frac{Ku_{n}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|M_{i}^{(1)}|.\end{split}

(A.117)

Next, we show that
\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}
is bounded on an event
E_{n}
with
\mathbb{P}(E_{n})\to 1
. Firstly, the local support of B-splines imply that

\begin{split}\left\|\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)^{\top}a\right\|_{\infty}&\leq K\|a\|_{\infty}\max_{1\leq k\leq K_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(k)}\Delta_{n}\\
&\leq K\|a\|_{\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}B_{t}^{(k)}\left(\Bigl\lceil\frac{|\text{supp}(B^{(k)})|}{\Delta_{n}}\Bigr\rceil+1\right)\Delta_{n}\leq\frac{K^{\prime}}{K_{n}},\end{split}

(A.118)

where
|\text{supp}(B^{(k)})|\asymp K_{n}^{-1}
; see Appendix
A.1
(iii). We define a permutation matrix
\mathbf{P}
that reorders elements in
\mathbf{R}
(group the
p
components associated with each spline basis index). Specifically, the
i
-th column in the row-permutated
\mathbf{P}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{pK_{n}\times n}
is given by

\begin{pmatrix}B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(1)}\Delta_{1,i}^{n}X\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
\vdots\\
B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(1)}\Delta_{p,i}^{n}X\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
\vdots\\
\vdots\\
B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(K_{n})}\Delta_{1,i}^{n}X\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
\vdots\\
B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(K_{n})}\Delta_{p,i}^{n}X\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{pK_{n}}.

(A.119)

Then we consider a rescaled and permutated Gram matrix
\mathbf{G}=K_{n}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})\mathbf{P}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{pK_{n}\times pK_{n}}
. By
Eq.

A.32
, the eigenvalues of
\mathbf{G}
are bounded away from 0 and infinity with probability approaching one, i.e.,

\mathbb{P}(E_{n})\to 1,\qquad\text{where }E_{n}=\{0<\underline{\kappa}\leq\lambda_{\text{min}}(\mathbf{G})\leq\lambda_{\text{max}}(\mathbf{G})\leq\overline{\kappa}<\infty\}.

(A.120)

Importantly, the local support property of B-spline basis implies that our rescaled matrix
\mathbf{G}
is banded with fixed bandwidth, i.e., we have
\mathbf{G}_{ij}=0
when
|i-j|>L
for some
L>0
.
8
8

8

More strictly speaking, the matrix
\mathbf{G}
is block-banded with block size
p
. When
p
is fixed, this block-bandedness automatically implies ordinary (scalar) bandedness by taking a half-bandwidth
L
large enough to cover the finitely many nonzero diagonal blocks.
A standard result for symmetric positive definite banded matrices from
Demko et al., 1984
is that the inverse has exponentially decaying off-diagonal entries. Specifically in our case, on
E_{n}
,
|(\mathbf{G}^{-1})_{ij}|\leq K\rho^{|i-j|}
for some
\rho\in(0,1)
, and therefore

\|\mathbf{G}^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{pK_{n}}|(\mathbf{G}^{-1})_{ij}|\leq K\left(1+2\sum_{m\geq 1}\rho^{m}\right)=K\left(1+\frac{2\rho}{1-\rho}\right)\leq\frac{K^{\prime}}{1-\rho}.

(A.121)

Therefore, with

\mathbf{P}\bm{w}_{n}=\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)^{\top}a=(K_{n}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})\mathbf{P}^{\top})^{-1}K_{n}\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)^{\top}a,

(A.122)

it holds that on
E_{n}
, by
Eqs.

A.118
and
A.121
,

\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbf{P}\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbf{G}^{-1}\|_{\infty}\left\|K_{n}\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)^{\top}a\right\|_{\infty}\leq K.

(A.123)

By
Eqs.

A.117
and
A.123
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[|H_{1,n}^{(1)}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]\leq\frac{Ku_{n}^{2}}{\Delta_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|M_{i}^{(1)}|\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K^{\prime}u_{n}^{2}}{\Delta_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|M_{i}^{(1)}|\right)^{2}\right],\end{split}

(A.124)

so it suffices to show

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|M_{i}^{(1)}|\right)^{2}\right]=o\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}}{u_{n}^{2}}\right),\qquad\text{such that}\quad\mathbb{E}[|H_{1,n}^{(1)}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]=o(1).

(A.125)

We define the subset of intervals,

\mathcal{I}_{n}=\{i:|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\text{ or }\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|>u_{n}\},

(A.126)

and apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality on that subset:

\begin{split}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|M_{i}^{(1)}|\right)^{2}=\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}|M_{i}^{(1)}|\right)^{2}\leq|\mathcal{I}_{n}|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}(M_{i}^{(1)})^{2}.\end{split}

(A.127)

Therefore, it holds that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|M_{i}^{(1)}|\right)^{2}\right]&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathcal{I}_{n}|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}(M_{i}^{(1)})^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathcal{I}_{n}|\left(|\mathcal{I}_{n}|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}(M_{i}^{(1)})^{4}\right)^{1/2}\right]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathcal{I}_{n}|^{3/2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(M_{i}^{(1)})^{4}\right)^{1/2}\right]\leq(\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{I}_{n}|^{3}])^{1/2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[(M_{i}^{(1)})^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq K(u_{n}^{-3r})^{1/2}(\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-r})^{1/2}=K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-2r}=o(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-2}),\end{split}

(A.128)

where
\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{I}_{n}|^{3}]=O(u_{n}^{-3r})
can be proved in a similar way to
Eq.

A.47
, and
\mathbb{E}[(M_{i}^{(1)})^{4}]=\Delta_{n}^{3}u_{n}^{-r}
can be verified by the same steps as in
Eq.

A.75
by the BDG inequality and
Lemma

A.2
(i).

Combining
Eqs.

A.124
and
A.128
leads to

\mathbb{E}[|H_{1,n}^{(1)}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]=o(1).

(A.129)

By Markov’s inequality, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}(|H_{1,n}^{(1)}|>\varepsilon)\leq\mathbb{P}(|H_{1,n}^{(1)}|>\varepsilon,E_{n})+\mathbb{P}(E_{n}^{\complement})\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[|H_{1,n}^{(1)}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\mathbb{P}(E_{n}^{\complement})=o(1),

(A.130)

and therefore
H_{1,n}^{(1)}=o_{p}(1)
.

(ii.2)
\bm{H_{1,n}^{(2)}=o_{p}(1)}

We follow the same steps as in (ii.1) from
Eq.

A.115
to
Eq.

A.125
, and it remains to show

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|M_{i}^{(2)}|\right)^{2}\right]=o\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}}{u_{n}^{2}}\right),\qquad\text{such that}\quad\mathbb{E}[|H_{1,n}^{(2)}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]=o(1).

(A.131)

We consider

|M_{i}^{(2)}|\leq K\underbrace{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{D_{i}},

(A.132)

so that it suffices to show

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\right)^{2}\right]=o\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}}{u_{n}^{2}}\right).

(A.133)

We consider the same event decomposition as in
Eqs.

A.58
,
A.59
and
A.60
:

\begin{split}\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}=\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n},\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq u_{n}\}\cup\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n},\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}.\end{split}

(A.134)

For each
i
, on the event
\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n},\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq u_{n}\}
,

\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|+\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq 2u_{n}.

(A.135)

Therefore,

D_{i}\,\leq\,\underbrace{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq 2u_{n}\}}}_{D_{i}^{(1)}}\,+\,\underbrace{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}}}_{D_{i}^{(2)}},

(A.136)

and also

\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\right)^{2}\leq 2\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{(1)}\right)^{2}+2\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{(2)}\right)^{2}.

(A.137)

For
D_{i}^{(1)}
, we follow the same steps in the proof of
Lemma

A.2
(ii) (see Eqs. (
A.61
) to (
A.65
)):

\begin{split}D_{i}^{(1)}&\leq\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\wedge 2u_{n}\leq\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx).\end{split}

(A.138)

Then we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{(1)}\right)^{2}\right]&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})(\mu-\nu)(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\qquad+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\nu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right].\end{split}

(A.139)

For the first term in
Eq.

A.139
, by the Itô isometry for compensated Poisson integrals,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})(\mu-\nu)(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})^{2}\nu(ds,dx)\right]\\
&\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}ds\left(\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq 2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|^{2}\lambda(dx)+4u_{n}^{2}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\lambda(dx)\right)\right]\leq Ku_{n}^{2-r},\end{split}

(A.140)

by
Eqs.

A.39
and
A.38
. For the second term in
Eq.

A.139
, by Jensen’s inequality,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\nu(ds,dx)\right)^{2}\right]\leq T\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\lambda(dx)\right)^{2}\right]ds\\
&\qquad\leq T\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq 2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\lambda(dx)+2u_{n}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\lambda(dx)\right)^{2}\right]ds\leq Ku_{n}^{2-2r},\end{split}

(A.141)

by
Eqs.

A.38
and
A.40
. Combining
Eqs.

A.139
,
A.140
and
A.141
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{(1)}\right)^{2}\right]\leq Ku_{n}^{2-r}+K^{\prime}u_{n}^{2-2r}\leq Ku_{n}^{2-2r}.

(A.142)

For
D_{i}^{(2)}
, on the event
\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n},\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}
,

\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq u_{n}+\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\leq 2\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|,

(A.143)

and thus it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{(2)}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[4n\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}}\right]

\displaystyle=4n\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}}\right].

(A.144)

For any
q>2
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|>u_{n}\}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\left(\frac{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|}{u_{n}}\right)^{q-2}\right]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{q}]}{u_{n}^{q-2}}\leq\Delta_{n}^{q/2}u_{n}^{2-q},

(A.145)

by the BDG inequality. Therefore,
Eq.

A.144
turns into

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{(2)}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\Delta_{n}^{q/2-2}u_{n}^{2-q}=o\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}}{u_{n}^{2}}\right),\qquad\text{if }q>\frac{3-4\varpi}{1/2-\varpi},

(A.146)

which is always possible because
\varpi<1/2
. By
Eqs.

A.137
,
A.142
and
A.146
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\right)^{2}\right]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{(1)}\right)^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{(2)}\right)^{2}\right]=o\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}}{u_{n}^{2}}\right),

(A.147)

and therefore

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|M_{i}^{(2)}|\right)^{2}\right]=o\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}}{u_{n}^{2}}\right),

(A.148)

so that
\mathbb{E}[|H_{1,n}^{(1)}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]=o(1)
. By the same inequality as in
Eq.

A.130
, we conclude
H_{1,n}^{(2)}=o_{p}(1)
.

(ii.3)
\bm{H_{1,n}^{(3)}=o_{p}(1)}

Write
H_{1,n}^{(3)}
into

H_{1,n}^{(3)}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{w}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}.

(A.149)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

|H_{1,n}^{(3)}|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{w}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i})^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

(A.150)

For the first multiplier on the right-hand side of
Eq.

A.150
,

\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{w}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i})^{2}&=\bm{w}_{n}^{\top}(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})\bm{w}_{n}=a^{\top}\left(\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\left(\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)^{\top}a\\
&\leq K\|a\|^{2}\underbrace{\left\|\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right\|^{2}}_{O(K_{n}^{-1})}\|(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\|=O_{p}(1),\end{split}

(A.151)

because each basis component
\sum^{n}_{i=1}B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(k)}\Delta_{n}\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}B_{t}^{(k)}\left(\Bigl\lceil\frac{|\text{supp}(B^{(k)})|}{\Delta_{n}}\Bigr\rceil+1\right)\Delta_{n}=O(K_{n}^{-1})
, and there are
K_{n}
of them in the Euclidean norm. For the other term, by
Eq.

A.34
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left.\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\leq K\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\|^{2}]ds.

(A.152)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\|\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\|^{2}\leq(s-(i-1)\Delta_{n})\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{s}\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}_{u}\|^{2}du\leq\Delta_{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}_{u}\|^{2}du.

(A.153)

Note that here we adopt a different approach from
Eq.

A.79
, and avoid using the probabilistic order bound for
\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}
. Then,

\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\|\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\|^{2}ds\leq\Delta_{n}^{2}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}_{u}\|^{2}du.

(A.154)

Combining
Eqs.

A.152
and
A.154
leads to

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left.\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\leq K\Delta_{n}^{2}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}_{u}\|^{2}]du,

(A.155)

and thus

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\right]\leq K\Delta_{n}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}_{u}\|^{2}]du=K\Delta_{n}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}].

(A.156)

Therefore, by Markov’s inequality,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}=O_{p}(\Delta_{n}^{2}\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

(A.157)

Combining
Eqs.

A.151
and
A.157
in
Eq.

A.150
, we obtain

|H_{1,n}^{(3)}|=O_{p}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}),

(A.158)

and it remains to derive the probabilistic order bound for
\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}
.

Lemma A.3
.

Under
2
,
\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}=O_{p}(\sqrt{K_{n}\log K_{n}})
.

Proof of
Lemma

A.3
.

Instead of working directly on
\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}
, we consider the quasi-interpolant
\overline{\beta}
defined in
Eq.

A.11
in the
proof
of
Lemma

1
, and we have

\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}=\overline{\beta}^{\prime}+(\widetilde{\beta}-\overline{\beta})^{\prime}.

(A.159)

with all
\widetilde{\beta}
,
\overline{\beta}
,
\widetilde{\beta}-\overline{\beta}\in\mathbb{G}_{n}
. By the triangle inequality,

\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\overline{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}+\|(\widetilde{\beta}-\overline{\beta})^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}.

(A.160)

We start with a general linear combination of B-spline basis functions of degree
d
: Let
g_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}B_{d,t}^{(k)}r^{(k)}\in\mathbb{G}_{n}
for any
r^{(k)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. By the Cox-de Boor recursion identity in
Eq.

A.3
,

(B_{d,t}^{(k)})^{\prime}=\frac{d}{\bar{v}_{k+d-1}-\bar{v}_{k-1}}B_{d-1,t}^{(k)}-\frac{d}{\bar{v}_{k+d}-\bar{v}_{k}}B_{d-1,t}^{(k+1)}.

(A.161)

Differentiate
g_{t}
on
t
with
Eq.

A.161
plugged in, we obtain

\displaystyle g^{\prime}_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}\frac{d}{\bar{v}_{k+d-1}-\bar{v}_{k-1}}B_{d-1,t}^{(k)}r^{(k)}-\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}\frac{d}{\bar{v}_{k+d}-\bar{v}_{k}}B_{d-1,t}^{(k+1)}r^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}\frac{d}{\bar{v}_{k+d-1}-\bar{v}_{k-1}}B_{d-1,t}^{(k)}r^{(k)}-\sum_{k=2}^{K_{n}+1}\frac{d}{\bar{v}_{k+d-1}-\bar{v}_{k-1}}B_{d-1,t}^{(k)}r^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{\bar{v}_{d}-\bar{v}_{0}}r^{(1)}+\sum_{k=2}^{K_{n}}\left(\frac{d}{\bar{v}_{k+d-1}-\bar{v}_{k-1}}r^{(k)}-\frac{d}{\bar{v}_{k+d-1}-\bar{v}_{k-1}}r^{(k-1)}\right)B_{d-1,t}^{(k)}-\frac{d}{\bar{v}_{K_{n}+d}-\bar{v}_{K_{n}}}r^{(K_{n})},

(A.162)

where the first and last terms are both 0 by definition; see
Eq.

A.4
.
Therefore,

g^{\prime}_{t}=\sum_{k=2}^{K_{n}}\frac{dB_{d-1,t}^{(k)}}{\bar{v}_{k+d-1}-\bar{v}_{k-1}}(r^{(k)}-r^{(k-1)})\leq K\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}(r^{(k+1)}-r^{(k)}),

(A.163)

and thus

\|g^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\int_{0}^{T}\|g^{\prime}_{t}\|^{2}dt\leq KK_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}\|r^{(k+1)}-r^{(k)}\|^{2}\leq K^{\prime}K_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}\|r^{(k)}\|^{2}=K^{\prime}K_{n}\|\bm{r}\|^{2},

(A.164)

where
\bm{r}=((r^{(1)})^{\top},\dots,(r^{(K_{n})})^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{pK_{n}}
. On the other hand, by the B-spline norm equivalence in Appendix
A.1
(iv),

\|g\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\asymp\frac{\|\bm{r}\|^{2}}{K_{n}}.

(A.165)

Combining
Eqs.

A.164
and
A.165
leads to

\|g^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\leq KK_{n}\|g\|_{L^{2}}.

(A.166)

Therefore, if
g=\widetilde{\beta}-\overline{\beta}
,

\|(\widetilde{\beta}-\overline{\beta})^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\leq KK_{n}\|\widetilde{\beta}-\overline{\beta}\|_{L^{2}}.

(A.167)

By the triangle inequality,
Eqs.

A.9
and
A.10
,

\|\widetilde{\beta}-\overline{\beta}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\beta-\overline{\beta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\beta-\widetilde{\beta}\|_{L^{2}}\leq K\|\beta-\overline{\beta}\|_{L^{2}}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log K_{n}}{K_{n}}}\right).

(A.168)

Therefore, by the above two inequalities,

\|(\widetilde{\beta}-\overline{\beta})^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}=O_{p}(\sqrt{K_{n}\log K_{n}}).

(A.169)

Then it remains to derive the probabilistic order bound for
\|\overline{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}
.

Let
g=\overline{\beta}
, by the first inequality in
Eq.

A.164
,

\|\overline{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq KK_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}\|\beta_{\tau^{(k+1)}}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}\|^{2}.

(A.170)

Consider the same decomposition as in
Eq.

A.12
. Then we have

\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}\|\beta_{\tau^{(k+1)}}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}\|^{2}\leq 2\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}\|\beta_{\tau^{(k+1)}}^{c}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}^{c}\|^{2}+2\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}\|\beta_{\tau^{(k+1)}}^{j}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}^{j}\|^{2}.

(A.171)

Under
A.1
, the BDG inequality implies that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}\|\beta_{\tau^{(k+1)}}^{c}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}^{c}\|^{2}\right]\leq K\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}(\tau^{(k+1)}-\tau^{(k)})\leq KT,

(A.172)

and thus, by Markov’s inequality,

\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}\|\beta_{\tau^{(k+1)}}^{c}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}^{c}\|^{2}=O_{p}(1).

(A.173)

Under
2
with finite-variation jumps,

\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}\|\beta_{\tau^{(k+1)}}^{j}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}^{j}\|^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}\|\beta_{\tau^{(k+1)}}^{j}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}^{j}\|\right)^{2}\leq V_{\beta^{j}}([0,T])^{2}=O_{p}(1),

(A.174)

where
V_{\beta^{j}}([a,b])
is defined in
Eq.

A.22
. Combining
Eqs.

A.173
and
A.174
in
Eq.

A.171
, we obtain

\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}-1}\|\beta_{\tau^{(k+1)}}-\beta_{\tau^{(k)}}\|^{2}=O_{p}(1),

(A.175)

and thus, by
Eq.

A.170
,

\|\overline{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}=O_{p}(\sqrt{K_{n}}).

(A.176)

Therefore, combining
Eqs.

A.169
and
A.176
in
Eq.

A.160
, we obtain

\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\leq O_{p}(\sqrt{K_{n}})+O_{p}(\sqrt{K_{n}\log K_{n}})=O_{p}(\sqrt{K_{n}\log K_{n}}).

(A.177)

This completes the proof of
Lemma

A.3
.

∎

Therefore, by
Eq.

A.158
and
Lemma

A.3
,

|H_{1,n}^{(3)}|=O_{p}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}K_{n}\log K_{n}})=o_{p}(1),

(A.178)

since
\Delta_{n}K_{n}\log K_{n}\to 0
as required, and thus
H_{1,n}^{(3)}=o_{p}(1)
.

By the results in (ii.1), (ii.2), and (ii.3), we conclude that
H_{1,n}=o_{p}(1)
.

(iii)
\bm{H_{2,n}=o_{p}(1)}

Recall that, with
\theta_{n,t}
defined in
Eq.

A.108
,

H_{2,n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\Delta_{i}^{n}X\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}.

(A.179)

On the high-probability event
E_{n}
in
Eq.

A.120
, it holds that
\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq K
by
Eq.

A.123
. By local support and boundedness of B-splines, on
E_{n}
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\theta_{n,t}\|=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}B_{t}^{(k)}\bm{w}_{n}\right\|\leq K\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq K^{\prime},

(A.180)

and
\|\theta^{\prime}_{n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(K_{n})
by
Eq.

A.79
. Consider
H_{2,n}=H_{2,n}^{(1)}+H_{2,n}^{(2)}
with

\displaystyle H_{2,n}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}},

(A.181)

\displaystyle H_{2,n}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c})\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}.

(A.182)

Next, we show both
H_{2,n}^{(1)}
and
H_{2,n}^{(2)}
are
o_{p}(1)
.

(iii.1)
\bm{H_{2,n}^{(1)}=o_{p}(1)}

Instead of working on
H_{2,n}^{(1)}
directly, we consider
H_{2,n}^{(1)}=\widetilde{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}-\overline{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right),

(A.183)

\displaystyle\overline{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\left(1-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\right),

(A.184)

and show that both
\widetilde{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}
and
\overline{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}
are
o_{p}(1)
.

We start with
\widetilde{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}
. Rewrite
Eq.

A.183
into
\widetilde{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}
with

U_{i}=\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right).

(A.185)

By the conditional covariance for continuous martingale increments (with the predictability justified in
Remark

A.1
via predictable projection),

\mathbb{E}[U_{i}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]=\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{\top}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}c_{s}e_{s}ds.

(A.186)

Consider

\begin{split}\widetilde{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}=\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[U_{i}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]}_{A_{2,n}^{(1)}}\,+\,\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(U_{i}-\mathbb{E}[U_{i}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}])}_{M_{2,n}^{(1)}}.\end{split}

(A.187)

For
A_{2,n}^{(1)}
, we have

A_{2,n}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\int_{0}^{T}\theta_{n,s}^{\top}c_{s}e_{s}ds-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\theta_{n,s}-\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}})^{\top}c_{s}e_{s}ds,

(A.188)

where the first term is 0 by
L^{2}(c)
-orthogonality between
e
and
\mathbb{G}_{n}
. For the second term, on
E_{n}
,

\sup_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}\|\theta_{n,s}-\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\|\leq\Delta_{n}\|\theta^{\prime}_{n}\|_{\infty}.

(A.189)

Therefore, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and bounded
c
under
A.1
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[|A_{2,n}^{(1)}|\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]&\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\|\theta_{n,s}-\theta_{n,(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\|\|e_{s}\|ds\right]\\
&\leq K\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}\mathbb{E}[\|\theta^{\prime}_{n}\|_{\infty}\|e\|_{L^{2}}]=O(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}K_{n}\log K_{n}})=o(1),\end{split}

(A.190)

and thus, by Markov’s inequality analogous to
Eq.

A.130
(but with first-order moment), we obtain
A_{2,n}^{(1)}=o_{p}(1)
. Moreover, for
M_{2,n}^{(1)}
, by the same steps as in
Eq.

A.75
, together with
Eq.

A.34
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[(M_{2,n}^{(1)})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]&\leq\frac{K}{\Delta_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[U_{i}^{2}]\leq\frac{K}{\Delta_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\leq\frac{K}{\Delta_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\leq K\mathbb{E}[\|e\|_{L^{2}}^{2}]=o(1),\end{split}

(A.191)

and thus
M_{2,n}^{(1)}=o_{p}(1)
by the same Markov’s inequality as in
Eq.

A.130
. Therefore, we conclude that
\widetilde{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}=A_{2,n}^{(1)}+M_{2,n}^{(1)}=o_{p}(1)
.

For
\overline{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}
, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[(\overline{H}_{2,n}^{(1)})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}\right]&\leq\frac{K}{\Delta_{n}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\left(1-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\right)\right]\right)\\
&\qquad\times\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\left(1-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\right)\right]\right).\end{split}

(A.192)

By the same steps as in
Eq.

A.75
, we obtain

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\left(1-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\right)\right]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}.

(A.193)

By
Eq.

A.34
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\leq K\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\|e_{s}\|^{2}ds,

(A.194)

and thus, by
Lemma

A.2
(i),

\begin{split}&\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\left(1-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\right)\right]\\
&\qquad\leq K\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\|e_{s}\|^{2}ds\right)\left(1-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\right)\right]\\
&\qquad\leq K\mathbb{E}[\|e\|_{L^{2}}^{2}]\max_{1\leq i\leq n}(\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})+\mathbb{P}(\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|>u_{n}))\\
&\qquad\leq K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}\mathbb{E}[\|e\|_{L^{2}}^{2}].\end{split}

(A.195)

Combining
Eqs.

A.193
and
A.195
in
Eq.

A.192
leads to

\mathbb{E}\left[(\overline{H}_{2,n}^{(1)})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}\right]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-2r}\mathbb{E}[\|e\|_{L^{2}}^{2}]=o(1).

(A.196)

By the same Markov’s inequality as in
Eq.

A.130
, we conclude
\overline{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}=o_{p}(1)
, and therefore

H_{2,n}^{(1)}=\widetilde{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}-\overline{H}_{2,n}^{(1)}=o_{p}(1).

(A.197)

(iii.2)
\bm{H_{2,n}^{(2)}=o_{p}(1)}

On
E_{n}
, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\mathbb{E}\left[(H_{2,n}^{(2)})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}\right]\leq\frac{K}{\Delta_{n}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\right]\right).

(A.198)

By
Lemma

A.2
(ii),

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\leq Ku_{n}^{2-r},

(A.199)

and, by
Eq.

A.34
,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\right]\leq K\mathbb{E}[\|e\|_{L^{2}}^{2}].

(A.200)

Combining
Eqs.

A.199
and
A.200
in
Eq.

A.198
, and by
Lemma

1
,

\mathbb{E}\left[(H_{2,n}^{(2)})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}\right]\leq\frac{K}{\Delta_{n}}u_{n}^{2-r}\mathbb{E}[\|e\|_{L^{2}}^{2}]=o(1),

(A.201)

which holds for all
u_{n}\asymp\Delta_{n}^{\varpi}
with
\varpi\in\left(\frac{1}{2(2-r)},\,\frac{1}{2}\right)
because

\frac{K}{\Delta_{n}}u_{n}^{2-r}\mathbb{E}[\|e\|_{L^{2}}^{2}]\asymp\Delta_{n}^{\varpi(2-r)-1}\frac{\log K_{n}}{K_{n}}=\Delta_{n}^{\varpi(2-r)-1/2}\frac{\log K_{n}}{K_{n}\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}=o(1),

(A.202)

where
\log K_{n}\big/(K_{n}\sqrt{\Delta_{n}})\to 0
as required, and
\Delta_{n}^{\varpi(2-r)-1/2}\to 0
because
\varpi(2-r)-1/2>0
. Therefore, by the same Markov’s inequality as in
Eq.

A.130
,
H_{2,n}^{(2)}=o_{p}(1)
.

By the results in (iii.1) and (iii.2), we conclude that
H_{2,n}=o_{p}(1)
.

(iv)
\bm{H_{3,n}=o_{p}(1)}

By the same steps as in
Eq.

A.117
and the safe substitution in
Eq.

A.82
,

|H_{3,n}|\leq\frac{Ku_{n}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathbf{J}_{i}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}=\frac{Ku_{n}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|,

(A.203)

and

\mathbb{E}[|H_{3,n}|\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]\leq\frac{Ku_{n}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|\right],

(A.204)

so it suffices to show

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|\right]=O(u_{n}^{1-r})=o\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}{u_{n}}\right),\qquad\text{such that}\quad\mathbb{E}[|H_{3,n}|\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]=o(1).

(A.205)

Same as in
Eq.

A.84
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|&\leq K\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx)\right.\\
&\qquad+\left.\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
&=K\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx)\\
&\qquad+K\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}.\end{split}

(A.206)

Following the same steps as in
Eq.

A.64
,
