\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx)\right]\leq K\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\lambda(dx)\right]\\
&\qquad\leq K\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\{\|\delta\|\leq 2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\lambda(dx)+2u_{n}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\lambda(dx)\right]\leq K^{\prime}u_{n}^{1-r},\end{split}

(A.207)

by
Eqs.

A.38
and
A.40
. For the other term, by
Eqs.

A.87
,
A.90
,
A.92
,
A.93
and
A.94
,

\begin{split}&\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\qquad\leq K\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\leq K\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\qquad\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-2r}=o(u_{n}^{1-r}).\end{split}

(A.208)

Combining
Eqs.

A.207
and
A.208
in
Eq.

A.206
leads to
\mathbb{E}[\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|]=o(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}u_{n}^{-1})
, and thus
\mathbb{E}[|H_{3,n}|\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]=o(1)
. Therefore, by the same Markov’s inequality as in
Eq.

A.130
,
H_{3,n}=o_{p}(1)
.

(v)
\bm{H_{5,n}=o_{p}(1)}

By the same steps as in
Eq.

A.117
,

\displaystyle|H_{5,n}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Ku_{n}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\left|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq u_{n}\}}\right|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Ku_{n}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq u_{n}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n}\}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Ku_{n}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\bm{w}_{n}\|_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(u_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right).

(A.209)

Note that the additional truncation
\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}
is harmlessly included because the design matrix
\mathbf{R}
already includes that for covariate increments. Then,

\mathbb{E}[|H_{5,n}|\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]\leq\frac{Ku_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})+\frac{K^{\prime}u_{n}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right].

(A.210)

For the first term, by
Lemma

A.2
(i),

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})=O(u_{n}^{-r}),\quad\text{and thus}\quad\frac{Ku_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})=O\left(\frac{u_{n}^{2-r}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\right)=o(1),

(A.211)

because
\varpi(2-r)-1/2>0
.

Now we turn to the second term in
Eq.

A.210
. Intuitively, on the event
\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}
,
the cancellation between
\Delta_{i}^{n}Y
,
\Delta_{i}^{n}X
and
\Delta_{i}^{n}Z
imposes a restriction on the size of
\Delta_{i}^{n}Z
. To formally establish this, we write
\Delta_{i}^{n}Y
into

\Delta_{i}^{n}Y=\,\underbrace{\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\beta_{s-}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}}_{\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}}\,\,+\,\,\underbrace{\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}}_{\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(2)}}\,\,+\,\,\Delta_{i}^{n}Z.

(A.212)

On
\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}
, it holds that

\begin{split}u_{n}<|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|&=|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y-\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}-\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(2)}|\\
&\leq|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(2)}|\\
&=|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(2)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}|\\
&=|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|+|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|\\
&\leq u_{n}+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|+|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|,\end{split}

(A.213)

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}
is defined in
Eq.

A.82
. Based on the above result, we consider the event decomposition:

\begin{split}&\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}\\
&\qquad\subseteq\{u_{n}<|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq 3u_{n}\}\,\cup\,\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|+|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>2u_{n}\}.\end{split}

(A.214)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\mathbbm{1}_{\{u_{n}<|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq 3u_{n}\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|+|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>2u_{n}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\underbrace{3u_{n}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n})}_{(1)}\,+\,\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(1)\,+\,\underbrace{u_{n}\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\right)}_{(2)}\,+\,\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(3)}\,+\,\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(4)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\,\underbrace{u_{n}\mathbb{P}\left(|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\right)}_{(5)}\,+\,\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(6)}\,+\,\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(7)}.

(A.215)

Next, we verify that the above terms (1)–(7) are all
O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r})
.

Term (1): By the same result as in
Lemma

A.2
(i) for the one-dimensional Itô semimartingale
Z
,

3u_{n}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n})\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r}.

(A.216)

Term (2): By
Eq.

A.54
,

u_{n}\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\right)\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r}.

(A.217)

Term (3): By the same steps as in
Eq.

A.67
and the BDG inequality, for any
q\geq 2
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|\left(\frac{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|}{u_{n}}\right)^{q-1}\right]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{q}]}{u_{n}^{q-1}}\leq K\Delta_{n}^{q/2}u_{n}^{1-q},\end{split}

(A.218)

and it is
O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r})
if
q\geq 2(1-\varpi r)/(1-2\varpi)
, which is always possible for
1/2(2-r)<\varpi<1/2
and
0\leq r<1
.

Term (4): By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
Eqs.

A.96
and
A.54
, for any
q\geq 2
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]&\leq\left(\mathbb{E}[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\right)\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq K(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}\vee\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r})^{1/2}(\Delta_{n}^{q/2}u_{n}^{-q})^{1/2}\\
&\leq K\Delta_{n}^{1/2+q/4}u_{n}^{1-r/2-q/2}\vee K\Delta_{n}^{1+q/4}u_{n}^{-r-q/2}\\
&=O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r}),\end{split}

(A.219)

if we choose some

q\geq\frac{2(1-\varpi r)}{1-2\varpi}\bigvee\frac{4\varpi}{1-2\varpi},

(A.220)

which is always possible for
1/2(2-r)<\varpi<1/2
and
0\leq r<1
.

Term (5): By Markov’s inequality,

u_{n}\mathbb{P}\left(|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\right)\leq u_{n}\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|]}{u_{n}}\leq\mathbb{E}[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r},

(A.221)

which can be calculated following the same steps as in
Eqs.

A.207
and
A.208
.

Term (6): By Hölder’s inequality, for some
q\geq 2
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]&\leq\left(\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{q}]\right)^{1/q}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\right)\right)^{1-1/q}\\
&\leq K\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r})^{1-1/q}=K\Delta_{n}^{3/2-1/q}u_{n}^{r/q-r},\end{split}

(A.222)

by the BDG inequality, and it is
O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r})
if
q\geq 2(1-\varpi r)/(1-2\varpi)
.

Term (7): Same as in
Eq.

A.84
, we have

|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|\leq K(A_{i}^{(1)}+A_{i}^{(2)})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}},

(A.223)

where

\displaystyle A_{i}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(\|\delta(s,x)\|\wedge 2u_{n})\mu(ds,dx),

(A.224)

\displaystyle A_{i}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\int_{\{\|\delta\|>2u_{n}\}}\|\delta(s,x)\|\mu(ds,dx),

(A.225)

and thus,

\mathbb{E}\left[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]\leq K\mathbb{E}\left[A_{i}^{(1)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{A_{i}^{(1)}>\frac{u_{n}}{2K}\}}\right]+K\mathbb{E}\left[A_{i}^{(2)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{A_{i}^{(2)}>\frac{u_{n}}{2K}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right].

(A.226)

For the first expectation on the right-hand side, by
Eq.

A.86
,

\mathbb{E}\left[A_{i}^{(1)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{A_{i}^{(1)}>\frac{u_{n}}{2K}\}}\right]\leq\frac{2K}{u_{n}}\mathbb{E}[(A_{i}^{(1)})^{2}]\leq K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r}.

(A.227)

For the second expectation, we follow the same steps as in
Eq.

A.208
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[A_{i}^{(2)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{A_{i}^{(2)}>\frac{u_{n}}{2K}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]&\leq K^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{\{N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})\geq 1\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\leq K^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[(N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n}))^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{N_{i}^{n}(2u_{n})\geq 1\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\leq K^{\prime\prime}\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r}=o(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r}).\end{split}

(A.228)

Therefore, it holds that for the term (7):

\mathbb{E}\left[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r}.

(A.229)

Therefore, combining all terms (1)–(7) in
Eq.

A.215
leads to

\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{1-r}.

(A.230)

By
Eqs.

A.210
,
A.211
and
A.230
,
\mathbb{E}[|H_{5,n}|\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}]=o(1)
. By the same Markov’s inequality as in
Eq.

A.130
, we conclude that
H_{5,n}=o_{p}(1)
.

By results in (
i
) to (
v
), we complete the proof of
Eq.

A.104
. Next, we show in (
vi
) and (
vii
) that the second term (the bias induced by the discretization error) and the third term (the bias induced by the spline approximation error) in
Eq.

A.103
are both asymptotically negligible and thus have no impact on the CLT.

(vi) Discretization error

Consider the discretization error term in
Eq.

A.103
:

D_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds-\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\right)\bm{\gamma}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})ds.

(A.231)

By the Cramér-Wold device, we need to show that, for any fixed linear combination with
a\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, it holds that
|a^{\top}D_{n}|=o_{p}(1)
. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, since
\widetilde{\beta}
is absolutely continuous and has bounded derivative on
[0,T]
(same as in
Eq.

A.78
),

\begin{split}|a^{\top}D_{n}|&\leq\|a\|\|D_{n}\|\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\widetilde{\beta}_{s}-\widetilde{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})ds\right\|\\
&\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{s}\|\widetilde{\beta}_{u}^{\prime}\|du\right)ds\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\|\widetilde{\beta}_{u}^{\prime}\|du\\
&\leq K\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\beta}_{u}^{\prime}\|du\leq K\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}\sqrt{T}\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\leq K^{\prime}\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}\|\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(A.232)

By
Lemma

A.3
,

|a^{\top}D_{n}|=O_{p}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}K_{n}\log K_{n}})=o_{p}(1).

(A.233)

(vii) Approximation error

Define the matrix-valued process
\psi=c^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{p}
, where
\mathbf{I}_{p}
is an identity matrix of dimension
p
. Under
1
(iii), consider the same
L^{2}(c)
-orthogonal projection
\Pi_{n}\psi\in\mathbb{G}_{n}
, and then

\int_{0}^{T}e_{s}ds=\int_{0}^{T}e_{s}c_{s}\psi_{s}ds=\langle e,\psi\rangle_{c}=\langle e,\psi-\Pi_{n}\psi\rangle_{c}.

(A.234)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the norm equivalence in
Eq.

A.8
,

\begin{split}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\int_{0}^{T}e_{s}ds\right\|&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|\langle e,\psi-\Pi_{n}\psi\rangle_{c}\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|e\|_{L^{2}(c)}\|\psi-\Pi_{n}\psi\|_{L^{2}(c)}\\
&\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\|e\|_{L^{2}}\|\psi-\Pi_{n}\psi\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(A.235)

By
Lemma

1
,
\|e\|_{L^{2}}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log K_{n}/K_{n}})
. Moreover,
\psi
is also an Itô semimartingale with finite-variation jumps (because
c
is bounded, nonsingular, and an Itô semimartingale under
1
(iii), so is
c^{-1}
by Itô’s formula for semimartingales with summable jumps; see, e.g., Theorem 3.2.2,
Jacod and Protter, 2012
), and then the result in
Lemma

1
also applies to
\psi-\Pi_{n}\psi
. Therefore,

\|\psi-\Pi_{n}\psi\|_{L^{2}}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log K_{n}}{K_{n}}}\right),

(A.236)

and then

\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\int_{0}^{T}e_{s}ds\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{\log K_{n}}{K_{n}\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\right)=o_{p}(1).

(A.237)

This completes the proof.

A.5
Proof of
Theorem

3

By the standard Riemann approximation and the LLN for the empirical Gram matrix in
Eq.

A.30
,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{B}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\Delta_{n}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds,\qquad(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})^{-1}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{s}^{\top}c_{s}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds\right)^{-1}.

(A.238)

Hence it suffices to show

\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{R}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\int_{0}^{T}\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}^{2}\mathbf{B}_{s}^{\top}c_{s}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds

(A.239)

We define the infeasible and feasible residuals:

\varepsilon_{i}=\Delta_{i}^{n}Y-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-},\qquad\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i}=\Delta_{i}^{n}Y-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)},

(A.240)

and
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}=\text{diag}(\Delta_{n}^{-1}\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}})
as the infeasible counterpart of
\mathbf{D}
. We first show that the difference between
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}
are asymptotically negligible, i.e.,

\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\mathbf{D}-\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\mathbf{R}\|=o_{p}(1).

(A.241)

Firstly, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i}^{2}-\varepsilon_{i}^{2}=(\Delta_{i}^{n}Y-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)})^{2}-(\Delta_{i}^{n}Y-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-})^{2}

\displaystyle=(2\Delta_{i}^{n}Y-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}-(\Delta_{i}^{n}Y-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-})((\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}(\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-}-\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}))

\displaystyle=(2\Delta_{i}^{n}Y-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-}+(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}(\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-}-\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}))((\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}(\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-}-\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}))

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon_{i}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}(\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-}-\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)})+((\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{\top}(\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-}-\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}))^{2},

(A.242)

and, by the BDG inequality and
Eq.

A.39
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[(\varepsilon_{i})^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\leq K\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s}-\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-})^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}+\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}\right)^{4}\right]\\
&\quad+K^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}\left(\Delta Y_{s}+(\beta_{s}^{J}-\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}\right)\right)^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\leq K\Delta_{n}^{2}+K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{4-r}.\end{split}

(A.243)

By the triangle inequality,

\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\mathbf{D}-\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\mathbf{R}\|\leq S_{1}+S_{2},

(A.244)

where, by
Eq.

A.242
and the boundedness of B-spline basis functions,

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{K}{\Delta_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\varepsilon_{i}|\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|^{3}\|\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-}-\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}\|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}},

(A.245)

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{K}{\Delta_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|^{4}\|\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-}-\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}},

(A.246)

and it suffices to show that both
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are
o_{p}(1)
.

Fix
i
and define the enlarged
\sigma
-field

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}=\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\vee\sigma\{(\Delta_{\ell}^{n}X,\Delta_{\ell}^{n}Y):\ell\neq i\}.

(A.247)

By construction,
\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}
-measurable. By
Lemma

A.2
(ii),

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]&\leq K\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{4}+\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\leq K\Delta_{n}^{2}+Ku_{n}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{c}\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\leq K\Delta_{n}^{2}+K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{4-r}\leq K^{\prime\prime}\Delta_{n}^{2},\end{split}

(A.248)

and thus

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[S_{2}]&=\frac{K}{\Delta_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\|\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-}-\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}\|^{2}\right]\\
&\leq K\Delta_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}-}-\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}\|^{2}\right]\asymp\mathbb{E}\left[\|\widehat{\beta}^{-}-\beta\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]=o(1),\end{split}

(A.249)

where
\Delta_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\beta_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}-\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}\|^{2}
is a Riemann sum for
\|\widehat{\beta}^{-}-\beta\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
of the piecewise constant process
\widehat{\beta}^{-}
that equals
\widehat{\beta}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(-i)}
on
((i-1)\Delta_{n},i\Delta_{n}]
, and the proof of
Theorem

1
applies to each leave-one-out OLS fit. Therefore,
S_{2}=o_{p}(1)
by Markov’s inequality.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\mathbb{E}[S_{1}]\leq K(\mathbb{E}[S_{2}])^{1/2}\left(\Delta_{n}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\right)^{1/2},

(A.250)

where, by
Eqs.

A.243
and
A.248
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\qquad\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\varepsilon_{i}^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]\right)^{1/2}\\
&\qquad\leq K\Delta_{n}^{2}+K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}^{3/2}u_{n}^{2-r/2}\leq K^{\prime\prime}\Delta_{n}^{2},\end{split}

(A.251)

and thus
S_{1}=o_{p}(1)
by Markov’s inequality. Therefore, it holds that
\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\mathbf{D}-\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\mathbf{R}\|=o_{p}(1)
, and thus

\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}+o_{p}(1).

(A.252)

Based on the same arguments on negligible remainders as in the proofs of
Theorems

1
and
2
, we have
\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\text{diag}(\Delta_{n}^{-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq u_{n}\}})\mathbf{R}+o_{p}(1)
, and thus, by the standard LLN result of truncated covariances,

\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\int_{0}^{T}\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}^{2}\mathbf{B}_{s}^{\top}c_{s}\mathbf{B}_{s}ds.

(A.253)

Combining
Eqs.

A.238
,
A.252
and
A.253
leads to the consistency of
\widehat{\Sigma}_{T}^{\beta}
. This completes the proof.

A.6
Proof of
Theorem

4

Define the block-wise score
c_{j}(\bm{\gamma})=\frac{\partial Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}=-(\mathbf{R}_{j}^{*})^{\top}(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{R}\bm{\gamma})
. The standard KKT conditions (e.g.,
Xue and Qu, 2012
, Eqs. (19)–(20)) imply that any local minimizer of
Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})
must satisfy:

\displaystyle c_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}})=0,\qquad\sqrt{\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}}>\tau_{n},\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for }j=1,\dots,q.

(A.254)

\displaystyle\sqrt{(c_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}))^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}c_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}})}\leq\frac{\lambda_{n}}{\tau_{n}},\qquad\sqrt{\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}}=0,\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for }j=q+1,\dots,p.

(A.255)

Then it suffices to show that
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}
satisfies both
Eqs.

A.254
and
A.255
with probability approaching one.

We denote by
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{q}^{\circ}
(resp.
\bm{\gamma}_{q}^{\circ}
) the subvector of
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}=((\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{q}^{\circ})^{\top},(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{-q}^{\circ})^{\top})^{\top}
(resp.
\bm{\gamma}^{\circ}
) that includes only the first
qK_{n}
elements, and by
\mathbf{R}_{q}=(\mathbf{R}_{1}^{*},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{q}^{*})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times qK_{n}}
the submatrix of
\mathbf{R}
corresponding to the first
q
regressors. Define the oracle least-squares spline estimator,

\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{q}^{\circ}=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\bm{\gamma}:\,\gamma_{j}\equiv 0,\,\forall q<j\leq p}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma}),\qquad\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{-q}^{\circ}\equiv 0,\qquad\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{q}^{\circ}=(\mathbf{R}_{q}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{q})^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{q}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}.

(A.256)

Define two standard projection matrices: the orthogonal projection matrix
\mathbf{H}_{q}=\mathbf{R}_{q}(\mathbf{R}_{q}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{q})^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{q}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
onto span(
\mathbf{R}_{q}
), and the residualizer
\mathbf{M}_{q}=\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{H}_{q}
, where
\mathbf{I}_{n}
is an identity matrix of dimension
n
. Both
\mathbf{H}_{q}
and
\mathbf{M}_{q}
are symmetric and idempotent. Then the oracle residual is given by

\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{R}\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}=\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{R}_{q}\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{q}^{\circ}=\mathbf{M}_{q}\mathbf{Y}.

(A.257)

Active blocks

By definition,
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}
minimizes
Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})
over the restricted model
\{\bm{\gamma}:\bm{\gamma}_{-q}\equiv 0\}
. Hence
c_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})=0
holds for all
1\leq j\leq q
. Same as
Eq.

A.29
, it holds that

(\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\int_{0}^{T}(\widehat{\beta}_{j,s}^{\circ})^{\top}c_{s}\widehat{\beta}_{j,s}^{\circ}ds\asymp\|\widehat{\beta}_{j}^{\circ}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\qquad\text{where}\quad\widehat{\beta}_{j}^{\circ}=\mathbf{B}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}.

(A.258)

By
Theorem

1
and the triangle inequality, for any active block
1\leq j\leq q
,

\left|\|\widehat{\beta}_{j}^{\circ}\|_{L^{2}}-\|\beta_{j}\|_{L^{2}}\right|\leq\|\widehat{\beta}_{j}^{\circ}-\beta_{j}\|_{L^{2}}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}0.

(A.259)

so
\sqrt{(\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\|\beta_{j}\|_{L^{2}}>0
, and thus it satisfies
\sqrt{(\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}}>\tau_{n}
for any
\tau_{n}\to 0
. Hence the active-block KKT conditions hold.

Inactive blocks

For
q+1\leq j\leq p
,
\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}\equiv 0
by construction. Under the condition

\frac{\lambda_{n}}{\tau_{n}}\bigg/\left(\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{n}\log pK_{n}}{K_{n}}}+u_{n}\log pK_{n}+u_{n}^{1-r/2}\right)\to\infty,

(A.260)

it suffices to show

\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\sqrt{(c_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}))^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}c_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{n}\log pK_{n}}{K_{n}}}+u_{n}\log pK_{n}+u_{n}^{1-r/2}\right),

(A.261)

which implies
Eq.

A.255
. By
Eq.

A.257
, we have the residualized score for any inactive blocks,

c_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})=-(\mathbf{R}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{q}\mathbf{Y}=-(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{Y},\qquad\text{where}\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*}=\mathbf{M}_{q}\mathbf{R}_{j}^{*},

(A.262)

for any
q+1\leq j\leq p
. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\sqrt{(c_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}))^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}c_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})}=\|\mathbf{W}_{j}^{1/2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{Y}\|\leq\|\mathbf{W}_{j}^{1/2}\|\|(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{Y}\|,

(A.263)

where
\|\mathbf{W}_{j}^{1/2}\|=\sqrt{\lambda_{\text{max}}(\mathbf{W}_{j})}=O_{p}(K_{n}^{-1/2})
as shown following
Eq.

A.32
. It suffices to show

\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\|(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{Y}\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}\log pK_{n}}+\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}\log pK_{n}+\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}^{1-r/2}\right).

(A.264)

We consider the decomposition of
\mathbf{Y}
as in
Eq.

A.33
(with the first two components combined):

\begin{split}\mathbf{Y}_{i}&=\underbrace{\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\beta_{s-}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}}+\,\underbrace{\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{\mathbf{J}_{i}}\\
&\qquad\quad+\,\underbrace{\Delta_{i}^{n}Z\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}}.\end{split}

(A.265)

Therefore, it suffices to verify, for each
\Xi\in\{\mathbf{C},\,\mathbf{J},\,\mathbf{Z}\}
,

\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\|(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\Xi\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}\log pK_{n}}+\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}\log pK_{n}+\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}^{1-r/2}\right).

(A.266)

We firstly show
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*}\|_{\infty}
is bounded for any
q+1\leq j\leq p
.

(i) Bounded
\bm{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*}\|_{\infty}}

Let
h_{ii}=(\mathbf{H}_{q})_{ii}
and
h_{\text{max}}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}h_{ii}
. For any
\bm{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
|(\mathbf{H}_{q}\bm{r})_{i}|\leq\sqrt{h_{ii}}\|\bm{r}\|
, and thus
\|\mathbf{H}_{q}\bm{r}\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{h_{\text{max}}}\|\bm{r}\|
. By definition,

\|\mathbf{M}_{q}\bm{r}\|_{\infty}\leq\|(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{H}_{q})\bm{r}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\bm{r}\|_{\infty}+\sqrt{h_{\text{max}}}\|\bm{r}\|.

(A.267)

Moreover, it holds that

\begin{split}h_{\text{max}}&\leq\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\mathbf{R}_{i}\|^{2}\right)\|(\mathbf{R}_{q}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{q})^{-1}\|\\
&\leq\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}(B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(k)}\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X|\leq u_{n}\}}\right)\|(\mathbf{R}_{q}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{q})^{-1}\|\\
&\leq\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq n}qu_{n}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{n}}(B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(k)})^{2}\right)\|(\mathbf{R}_{q}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{q})^{-1}\|=O_{p}(K_{n}u_{n}^{2}),\end{split}

(A.268)

by the boundedness and local support of B-splines, and
\|(\mathbf{R}_{q}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{q})^{-1}\|=O_{p}(K_{n})
. Therefore, by
Eqs.

A.267
and
A.268
,

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbf{R}_{j}^{*}\|_{\infty}+\sqrt{h_{\text{max}}}\|\mathbf{R}_{j}^{*}\|\leq Ku_{n}+K^{\prime}\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{K_{n}}}\leq K^{\prime\prime}u_{n}.

(A.269)

(ii) Maximal inequality for martingale difference array

We start with a high-dimensional martingale maximal inequality from standard Freedman’s inequality, as an analogue to Lemma A.1 in
van de Geer, 2008
from Bernstein’s inequality:

Lemma A.4
.

Let
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i=0}^{n}
be a filtration and
(M_{i}^{(\ell)})
be a triangular array indexed by
1\leq\ell\leq m
. Assume there exist
a_{n}
,
b_{n}>0
such that for all
1\leq\ell\leq m
,

(i)

\mathbb{E}[M_{i}^{(\ell)}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]=0
for all
i
;

(ii)

|M_{i}^{(\ell)}|\leq b_{n}
almost surely for all
i
;

(iii)

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[(M_{i}^{(\ell)})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\leq a_{n}
.

Then,

\max_{1\leq\ell\leq m}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{i}^{(\ell)}\right|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{a_{n}\log m}+b_{n}\log m\right).

(A.270)

Proof of
Lemma

A.4
.

By Freedman’s inequality (see, e.g., Theorem 1.1,
Tropp, 2011
), for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{i}^{(\ell)}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2(a_{n}+b_{n}\varepsilon/3)}\right).

(A.271)

By the union bound over all
1\leq\ell\leq m
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq\ell\leq m}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{i}^{(\ell)}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)\leq 2m\exp\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2(a_{n}+b_{n}\varepsilon/3)}\right).

(A.272)

Choose
\varepsilon=C(\sqrt{a_{n}\log m}+b_{n}\log m)
with a sufficiently large constant
C>0
.

(a) If
\sqrt{a_{n}\log m}\geq b_{n}\log m
, we have
\varepsilon\asymp C\sqrt{a_{n}\log m}
,
\sqrt{a_{n}}\geq b_{n}\sqrt{\log m}
, and
a_{n}+b_{n}\varepsilon/3\asymp a_{n}
, so the right-hand side of
Eq.

A.272
satisfies

2m\exp\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2(a_{n}+b_{n}\varepsilon/3)}\right)\leq 2m\exp(-KC^{2}\log m)=2m^{1-KC^{2}}\to 0,

(A.273)

for
C
large enough.

(b) If
\sqrt{a_{n}\log m}\leq b_{n}\log m
, we have
\varepsilon\asymp Cb_{n}\log m
,
a_{n}\leq b_{n}^{2}\log m
, and
a_{n}+b_{n}\varepsilon/3\asymp b_{n}^{2}\log m
, so the right-hand side of
Eq.

A.272
has the same bound as in
Eq.

A.273
.

This completes the proof of
Lemma

A.4
.

∎

Next, we use
Lemma

A.4
to prove
Eq.

A.266
for each
\Xi
. We start with some notation: For the
j
-th block design submatrix
\mathbf{R}_{j}^{*}
in
Eq.

30
, we write each element as

R_{j,k,i}=B_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{(k)}\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X|\leq u_{n}\}},

(A.274)

and the
((j-1)K_{n}+k)
-column
R_{j,k}=(R_{j,k,i})_{i=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*}=\mathbf{M}_{q}\mathbf{R}_{j}^{*}
, we write the corresponding “residualized” column as

\widetilde{R}_{j,k}=\mathbf{M}_{q}R_{j,k}=(\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i})_{i=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

(A.275)

where each element
|\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}|\leq Ku_{n}
by
Eq.

A.269
.

(ii.1)
\bm{\Xi=\mathbf{Z}}

Define

\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}=\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}\Delta_{i}^{n}Z\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}},

(A.276)

and

M_{i}^{(j,k)}=\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right],\quad\text{so that}\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left.M_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]=0.

(A.277)

With finite-variation jumps in
Z
and bounded
\widetilde{\delta}
under
A.1
, it holds that
|\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}|\leq Ku_{n}
, and thus
|M_{i}^{(j,k)}|\leq 2Ku_{n}
. For the conditional variance, by Itô’s isometry and Markov’s inequality,
\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z)^{2}|\breve{\mathcal{F}}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]=O_{p}(\Delta_{n})
with an enlarged
\sigma
-field
\breve{\mathcal{F}}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}=\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\cup\sigma(\mathbf{M}_{q},\Delta_{i}^{n}X)
. Then, since
\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}
is
\breve{\mathcal{F}}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}
-measurable, by the law of iterated conditional expectations,

\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(M_{i}^{(j,k)})^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)})^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\\
&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z)^{2}\right|\breve{\mathcal{F}}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\\
&\leq K\Delta_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right],\end{split}

(A.278)

where, since
\mathbf{M}_{q}
is an orthogonal projection,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{R}_{j,k}\|^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|R_{j,k}\|^{2}]=O(K_{n}^{-1}),

(A.279)

and thus
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[(M_{i}^{(j,k)})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]=O_{p}(K_{n}^{-1}\Delta_{n})
by Markov’s inequality.
Therefore, by
Lemma

A.4
,

\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\max_{1\leq k\leq K_{n}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{n}\log pK_{n}}{K_{n}}}+u_{n}\log pK_{n}\right).

(A.280)

Next, we bound the predictable compensator uniformly. Write

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}\Delta_{i}^{n}Z\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]

(A.281)

By the exogeneity condition under
3
, the untruncated product has zero conditional expectation:
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]=0
. Hence, we flip the indicator function

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]=-\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}\Delta_{i}^{n}Z\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right],\end{split}

(A.282)

and then, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
Lemma

A.2
(i), and Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq Ku_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq Ku_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z)^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq K^{\prime}u_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}O_{p}(\Delta_{n})\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}O_{p}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r})\right)^{1/2}=O_{p}(u_{n}^{1-r/2}),

(A.283)

for any
q+1\leq j\leq p
and
1\leq k\leq K_{n}
. Combining
Eqs.

A.280
and
A.283
in
Eq.

A.277
, we obtain

\begin{split}\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\|(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\|&\leq\sqrt{K_{n}}\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\max_{1\leq k\leq K_{n}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|\\
&\leq\sqrt{K_{n}}\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\max_{1\leq k\leq K_{n}}\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|+\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\right|\right)\\
&=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}\log pK_{n}}+\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}\log pK_{n}+\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}^{1-r/2}\right).\end{split}

(A.284)

(ii.2)
\bm{\Xi=\mathbf{C}}

Define

\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}=\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\beta_{s-}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}},

(A.285)

and
M_{i}^{(j,k)}=\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]
.
By bounded
\beta
under
A.1
,
|M_{i}^{(j,k)}|\leq Ku_{n}
. The remaining steps follow exactly as in (
ii.1
), and we conclude

\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\|(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{C}\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}\log pK_{n}}+\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}\log pK_{n}+\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}^{1-r/2}\right).

(A.286)

(ii.3)
\bm{\Xi=\mathbf{J}}

Using the safe replacement
\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}
for
\mathbf{J}
in
Eq.

A.82
,

\begin{split}\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}&=\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\\
&=\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}},\end{split}

(A.287)

since
\widetilde{R}_{j,k,i}
already includes the truncation
\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X|\leq u_{n}\}}
, and
M_{i}^{(j,k)}=\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]
. With bounded
\beta^{J}
and
\delta
under
A.1
and finite-variation jumps in
X
,
|M_{i}^{(j,k)}|\leq Ku_{n}
. For the conditional variance, we follow the same steps as in (
ii.1
). By the result in
Eq.

A.96
and Markov’s inequality,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}\right|\breve{\mathcal{F}}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]=O_{p}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}+\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r}),

(A.288)

and thus
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[(M_{i}^{(j,k)})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}]=O_{p}(K_{n}^{-1}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}+\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r}))
. Therefore, by
Lemma

A.4
,

\begin{split}\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\max_{1\leq k\leq K_{n}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|&=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}+\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r})\log pK_{n}}{K_{n}}}+u_{n}\log pK_{n}\right)\\
&=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{n}\log pK_{n}}{K_{n}}}+u_{n}\log pK_{n}\right).\end{split}

(A.289)

We bound the predictable compensator uniformly with the flipped indicator function as in
Eq.

A.283
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\widetilde{M}_{i}^{(j,k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq Ku_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left|\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}\right|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{j,i}^{n}X|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq K^{\prime}u_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}\Delta X_{s}\right)^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}|\mathcal{F}_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad=O_{p}(u_{n}^{1-r/2}).

(A.290)

Combining
Eqs.

A.289
and
A.290
, as in
Eq.

A.284
, leads to

\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\|(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{J}\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}\log pK_{n}}+\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}\log pK_{n}+\sqrt{K_{n}}u_{n}^{1-r/2}\right).

(A.291)

Finally,
Eq.

A.266
holds by all results in (
ii.1
), (
ii.2
) and (
ii.3
), and this completes the proof.

A.7
Proof of
Theorem

5

We define the oracle index set
A^{\circ}=\{1,\dots,q\}
. Let
\Gamma_{1}=\{A:A\subseteq\{1,\dots,p\},A^{\circ}\subset A,A\neq A^{\circ}\}
collect all index sets of overfitting models,
\Gamma_{2}=\{A:A\subseteq\{1,\dots,p\},A\subset A^{\circ},A\neq A^{\circ}\}
collect those of underfitting models, and
\Gamma_{3}=\{A:A\subseteq\{1,\dots,p\},A^{\circ}\not\subset A,A\not\subset A^{\circ},A\neq A^{\circ}\}
collect those of other misspecified models. For any
\bm{\gamma}
,
A(\bm{\gamma})=\{j:\sqrt{\gamma_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\gamma_{j}}\geq\tau_{n}\}
must fall into one of
\Gamma_{\ell}
for
\ell\in\{1,2,3\}
if
A(\bm{\gamma})\neq A^{\circ}
.
|A|
denotes the cardinality of
A
. We aim to show that, with probability approaching one,

\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})\neq A^{\circ}}Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})>Q_{n}^{*}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}),

(A.292)

which ensures that the global minimizer
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{*}
of
Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})
coincides with the oracle estimator
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}
. It suffices to show that, for
\ell\in\{1,2,3\}
,

\sum_{A\in\Gamma_{\ell}}\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})-Q_{n}^{*}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})\leq 0\right)\to 0,

(A.293)

such that, combining with
Theorem

4
, it holds that the global minimizer
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{*}
exists and satisfies
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{*}=\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}
with probability approaching one. Firstly, we have

\begin{split}\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})&=\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}\left(\frac{1}{2}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})+\lambda_{n}\sum_{j\in A(\bm{\gamma})}\rho_{n}\left(\sqrt{\gamma_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\gamma_{j}}\right)+\lambda_{n}\sum_{j\in\{1,\dots,p\}\backslash A(\bm{\gamma})}\rho_{n}\left(\sqrt{\gamma_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\gamma_{j}}\right)\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})+\lambda_{n}|A|,\end{split}

(A.294)

and thus comparing any model
A
to the oracle model
A^{\circ}
reduces to comparing unpenalized losses and constant penalties:

\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})-Q_{n}^{*}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})=\frac{1}{2}\left(\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})-Q_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})\right)+\lambda_{n}(|A|-q).

(A.295)

For any index set
A
, let
\mathbf{R}_{A}=(\mathbf{R}_{j}^{*})_{j\in A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K_{n}|A|}
be the submatrix of
\mathbf{R}
formed by blocks
j\in A
. We define the orthogonal projection matrix
\mathbf{H}_{A}=\mathbf{R}_{A}(\mathbf{R}_{A}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{A})^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{A}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
onto span(
\mathbf{R}_{A}
), and the residualizer
\mathbf{M}_{A}=\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{H}_{A}
, where
\mathbf{I}_{n}
is an identity matrix of dimension
n
. Both
\mathbf{H}_{A}
and
\mathbf{M}_{A}
are symmetric and idempotent.

Overfitting models

For any overfitting models with
A\in\Gamma_{1}
, the added blocks in the design matrix
\mathbf{R}_{A}
corresponding to the index set
A\backslash A^{\circ}
leads to a decrease in the OLS residual sums of squares. We denote the residual vector by
\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}=\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{R}\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}=\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{R}_{A^{\circ}}\bm{\gamma}_{A^{\circ}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For any irrelevant group
j>q
, we define the spurious improvement in the unpenalized loss from adding block
j
as

\Delta Q_{n,j}=Q_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})-\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})\subseteq A^{\circ}\cup\{j\}}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}\cup\{j\}})\mathbf{Y}.

(A.296)

The following
Lemma

A.5
provides a uniform bound for
\Delta Q_{n,j}
:

Lemma A.5
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

5
, there exists a constant
K>0
independent of
n
and
p
such that, as
\Delta_{n}\to 0
,

\mathbb{P}(E_{n})=\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\Delta Q_{n,j}\leq K\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)\right)\to 1.

(A.297)

Proof of
Lemma

A.5
.

Define the residualized block as
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*}=\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{R}_{j}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K_{n}}
, the projection matrix
\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*}=\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*}((\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{-1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
onto span
(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})
, and the corresponding residualizer
\mathbf{M}_{j}^{*}=\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*}
. By the Frisch-Waugh-Lovell theorem (Theorem 2.1,
Davidson and MacKinnon, 2004
), the OLS residual sums of squares from the overfitted model with the irrelevant block
j
satisfies

\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}\cup\{j\}}\mathbf{Y}=\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{j}^{*}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}},

(A.298)

so that

\Delta Q_{n,j}=\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}-\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{j}^{*}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}=\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}=\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}\|^{2},

(A.299)

where
\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*}=\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*}((\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{*})^{-1/2}
, and it suffices to show

\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}\|^{2}\leq K\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p),

(A.300)

with probability approaching one.

Consider a further decomposition based on
Eq.

A.33
:

\begin{split}\mathbf{Y}_{i}&=\underbrace{\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\widetilde{\beta}_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}}\,+\,\underbrace{\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}}_{\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(1)}}\\
&\qquad-\,\underbrace{\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}}_{\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(2)}}\,+\,\underbrace{\left(\sum_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}\leq s\leq i\Delta_{n}}(\beta_{s-}^{J})^{\top}\Delta X_{s}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{\mathbf{J}_{i}}\\
&\qquad\quad+\,\underbrace{\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{(1)}}\,-\,\underbrace{\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{(2)}}\,+\,\underbrace{(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}}_{\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{(3)}}.\end{split}

(A.301)

We denote the leading continuous-martingale term by
\Theta=\mathbf{U}^{(1)}+\mathbf{Z}^{(1)}
, and all remainders by
\Xi\in\{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{R}_{A^{\circ}}\bm{\gamma}_{A^{\circ}},\,\mathbf{U}^{(2)},\,\mathbf{J},\,\mathbf{Z}^{(2)},\,\mathbf{Z}^{(3)}\}
. Then, it suffices to show

\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\Theta\|^{2}\leq K\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p),

(A.302)

with probability approaching one, and, for each
\Xi
,

\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\Xi\|^{2}=o_{p}(\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)).

(A.303)

We firstly verify
Eq.

A.302
. Since
\Theta
contains only continuous semimartingale increments,
\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\Theta\|^{2}
can be written into a quadratic form of (conditional) Gaussian random variables, and
Eq.

A.302
can be proved with the Hanson-Wright inequality (
Hanson and Wright, 1971
; Theorem 6.2.1,
Vershynin, 2018
). Specifically, we assume
b,\widetilde{b}\equiv 0
without loss of generality as the drift only contributes a negligible order of
\Delta_{n}
, and thus, conditional on a
\sigma
-field
\mathcal{G}=\sigma(c_{s},\widetilde{\sigma}_{s},e_{s}:0\leq s\leq T)
,
\Theta_{i}
are independent Gaussian variables with mean 0 and variances

\text{Var}(\Theta_{i}|\mathcal{G})=\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}c_{s}e_{s}ds+\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}^{2}ds=\bar{v}_{i}\Delta_{n},

(A.304)

for all
i=1,\dots,n
, with
0\leq\bar{v}_{i}\leq\overline{K}
for some
\overline{K}>0
under
A.1
. Therefore, we denote the conditional covariance matrix of
\Theta
by

\bm{\Sigma}=\text{Cov}(\Theta|\mathcal{G})=\text{diag}(\bar{v}_{1}\Delta_{n},\dots,\bar{v}_{n}\Delta_{n}),

(A.305)

and thus
\Theta=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\Gamma
, where
\Gamma
is a
n\times 1
vector of standard Gaussian random variables and is independent of the conditioning. Then, conditional on the same
\sigma
-field,
\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\Theta\|^{2}
can be written into a quadratic form of Gaussian random variables: With symmetric and idempotent
\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}
,

\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\Theta\|^{2}=\Gamma^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\Gamma=\Gamma^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\Gamma,\quad\text{with}\quad\bm{\Lambda}_{j}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}.

(A.306)

By definition of the block-wise orthogonal projection matrix,
\|\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*}\|=1
and rank
(\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*})=K_{n}
. By the submultiplicativity of operator norms,

\|\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\|\leq\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\|\|\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*}\|\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\|=\lambda_{\text{max}}(\bm{\Sigma})=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\bar{v}_{i}\Delta_{n}\leq\overline{K}\Delta_{n},

(A.307)

and for symmetric
\bm{\Lambda}_{j}
, with
\text{rank}(\bm{\Lambda}_{j})=\text{rank}(\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*})=K_{n}
,

\|\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\text{rank}(\bm{\Lambda}_{j})\|\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\|^{2}\leq\overline{K}^{2}K_{n}\Delta_{n}^{2}.

(A.308)

By the Hanson-Wright inequality, for any
t>0
and some constant
k>0
,

\mathbb{P}(|\Gamma^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\Gamma-\mathbb{E}[\Gamma^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\Gamma]|>t\,|\,\mathcal{G})\leq 2\exp\left(-k\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{\|\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\|_{F}^{2}},\,\frac{t}{\|\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\|}\right\}\right),

(A.309)

where

\mathbb{E}[\Gamma^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\Gamma]=\text{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{j})=\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})=\text{tr}(\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*}\mathbf{\Sigma})\leq\text{tr}(\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*})\|\bm{\Sigma}\|\leq K_{n}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|\leq\overline{K}K_{n}\Delta_{n}.

(A.310)

Let
t=\overline{K}\Delta_{n}(\sqrt{K_{n}c\log p}+c\log p)
for some constant
c>0
satisfying
kc>1
. Then,

\displaystyle\frac{t^{2}}{\|\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\|_{F}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\overline{K}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{2}(\sqrt{K_{n}c\log p}+c\log p)^{2}}{\overline{K}^{2}K_{n}\Delta_{n}^{2}}\geq\frac{(\sqrt{K_{n}c\log p}+c\log p)^{2}}{K_{n}}\geq c\log p,

(A.311)

\displaystyle\frac{t}{\|\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\|}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\overline{K}\Delta_{n}(\sqrt{K_{n}c\log p}+c\log p)}{\overline{K}\Delta_{n}}\geq c\log p.

(A.312)

Combining
Eqs.

A.310
,
A.311
and
A.312
in
Eq.

A.309
leads to

\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\Gamma^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\Gamma>\overline{K}\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\sqrt{K_{n}c\log p}+c\log p)\right|\mathcal{G}\right)\leq 2e^{-kc\log p}=2p^{-kc}.

(A.313)

Applying a union bound over all irrelevant blocks, we obtain

\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\Gamma^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\Gamma>\overline{K}\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\sqrt{K_{n}c\log p}+c\log p)\right|\mathcal{G}\right)\leq 2p^{1-kc}\to 0,

(A.314)

when
p\to\infty
and
1-kc<0
. Taking expectations on both sides implies the same union bound holds unconditionally. By
Eq.

A.306
, it holds that with probability approaching one,

\begin{split}\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\Theta\|^{2}\leq\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\Gamma^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\Gamma\leq\overline{K}\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\sqrt{K_{n}c\log p}+c\log p).\end{split}

(A.315)

Furthermore, with
\sqrt{K_{n}\log p}\leq(K_{n}+\log p)/2
,

\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\Theta\|^{2}\leq K\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p),

(A.316)

with probability approaching one, i.e.,
Eq.

A.302
holds.

Next, we show
Eq.

A.303
holds for each
\Xi\in\{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}},\,\mathbf{U}^{(2)},\,\mathbf{J},\,\mathbf{Z}^{(2)},\,\mathbf{Z}^{(3)}\}
. By definition,

\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\Xi\|^{2}=\Xi^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{*}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\Xi\leq\|\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\Xi\|^{2}\leq\|\Xi\|^{2}.

(A.317)

Next, we show
\|\Xi\|^{2}=o_{p}(\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p))
for each
\Xi
.

(i)
\bm{\Xi=\mathbf{Z}^{(2)}}

By definition,

\|\mathbf{Z}^{(2)}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}.

(A.318)

By Hölder’s and BDG inequalities, as well as
Lemma

A.2
(i), for any
q\geq 2
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}\right]&\leq\left(\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2q}]\right)^{1/q}\left(\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})\right)^{1-1/q}\\
&\leq K\Delta_{n}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r})^{1-1/q}=K\Delta_{n}^{2-1/q}u_{n}^{r/q-r},\end{split}

(A.319)

and it is
O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r})
if we choose
q\geq(1-\varpi r)/(1-2\varpi)
, which is always possible for
1/2(2-r)<\varpi<1/2
and
0\leq r<1
. By Markov’s inequality,

\|\mathbf{Z}^{(2)}\|^{2}=O_{p}(u_{n}^{2-r})=o_{p}(\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)),

(A.320)

when
\Delta_{n}^{1-\varpi(2-r)}(K_{n}+\log p)\to\infty
as required.

(ii)
\bm{\Xi=\mathbf{Z}^{(3)}}

By definition,

\|\mathbf{Z}^{(3)}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq u_{n}\}}+\left|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq u_{n}\}}\right|\right),

(A.321)

where, by
Lemma

A.2
(ii) and Markov’s inequality,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq u_{n}\}}=O_{p}(u_{n}^{2-r}),

(A.322)

and, following the same steps as in Appendix
A.4
(
v
) for truncation mismatch,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\left|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq u_{n}\}}\right|=O_{p}(u_{n}^{2-r}\vee\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-2r}).

(A.323)

Specifically, we denote
X^{\circ}=(X_{1,t},\dots,X_{q,t})^{\top}_{t\geq 0}
, and follow
Eq.

A.209
:

\begin{split}&\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\left|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n}\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq u_{n}\}}\right|\\
&\quad\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}(u_{n}-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\circ}\|\leq u_{n}\}}.\end{split}

(A.324)

Note that
X^{\circ}
has only a finite dimension of nonzero components (relevant regressors), so the fixed dimensional result in Appendix
A.4
(
v
) still holds. As in the previous proofs, the OLS regression in
Eq.

12
already incorporates the truncation indicator
\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\circ}\|\leq u_{n}\}}
, so it does no harm to include it in any
\Xi
. For the first sum in
Eq.

A.324
,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}(u_{n}-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{n}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}+2\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}}_{\|\mathbf{Z}^{(2)}\|^{2}}.

(A.325)

By
Lemma

A.2
(i) and Markov’s inequality,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{n}^{2}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})=O(u_{n}^{2-r})\quad\Rightarrow\quad\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{n}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}=O_{p}(u_{n}^{2-r}).

(A.326)

and, with the result for
\|\mathbf{Z}^{(2)}\|^{2}
in
Eq.

A.320
, the first sum in
Eq.

A.324
is
O_{p}(u_{n}^{2-r})
.

For the second sum, we follow the same steps as from
Eqs.

A.212
,
A.213
,
A.214
and
A.215
. On
\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\circ}\|\leq u_{n}\}
, it holds that, by
Eq.

A.213
,

u_{n}^{2}\leq|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|^{2}\leq(u_{n}+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|+|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|)^{2}\leq 3(u_{n}^{2}+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{2}+|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}),

(A.327)

and thus

|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\leq K(u_{n}^{2}+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{2}+|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}+|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}).

(A.328)

By the same event decomposition as in
Eq.

A.214
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\circ}\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{u_{n}<|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|\leq 3u_{n}\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|+|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>2u_{n}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\underbrace{(3u_{n})^{2}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n})}_{(1)}\,+\,2\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(2)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(1)+(2)\,+\,K\underbrace{u_{n}^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\right)}_{(3)}\,+\,K\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(4)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(5)}\,+\,K\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(6)}+K\underbrace{u_{n}^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\right)}_{(7)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(8)}\,+\,K\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(9)}\,+\,K\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]}_{(10)}.

(A.329)

Next, we verify that the above terms (1)–(10) are all
O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}\vee\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r})
.

Term (1): By the same result as in
Lemma

A.2
(i) for the one-dimensional Itô semimartingale
Z
,

u_{n}^{2}\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n})\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}.

(A.330)

Term (2): Following the same steps as in
Eq.

A.319
,

\mathbb{E}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n}\}}\right]=O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}).

(A.331)

Term (3): By
Eq.

A.54
,

u_{n}^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\right)\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}.

(A.332)

Term (4): By the same steps as in
Eq.

A.67
and the BDG inequality, for any
q\geq 2
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{2}\left(\frac{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|}{u_{n}}\right)^{q-2}\right]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{q}]}{u_{n}^{q-2}}\leq K\Delta_{n}^{q/2}u_{n}^{2-q},\end{split}

(A.333)

and it is
O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r})
if
q\geq 2(1-\varpi r)/(1-2\varpi)
, which is always possible for
1/2(2-r)<\varpi<1/2
and
0\leq r<1
.

Term (5): By
Eq.

A.96
,

\mathbb{E}\left[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}+K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r}.

(A.334)

Term (6): Following the same steps as in
Eq.

A.319
, together with
Eq.

A.54
,

\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|>u_{n}\}}\right]=O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}).

(A.335)

Term (7): By
Eq.

A.221
,

u_{n}^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\right)\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}.

(A.336)

Term (8): By Hölder’s and BDG inequalities, together with
Eq.

A.221
, for some
q\geq 2
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]&\leq\left(\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{(1)}|^{2q}]\right)^{1/q}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\right)\right)^{1-1/q}\\
&\leq K\Delta_{n}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r})^{1-1/q}=K\Delta_{n}^{2-1/q}u_{n}^{r/q-r},\end{split}

(A.337)

and it is
O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r})
if we choose
q\geq(1-\varpi r)/(1-2\varpi)
, which is always possible for
1/2(2-r)<\varpi<1/2
and
0\leq r<1
.

Term (9): Same as term (5), we have

\mathbb{E}\left[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}[|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|^{2}]\leq K\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}+K^{\prime}\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r}.

(A.338)

Term (10): Following the same steps as in
Eq.

A.319
, together with
Eq.

A.221
,

\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{i}|>u_{n}\}}\right]=O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}).

(A.339)

Combining all terms (1)–(10) in
Eq.

A.329
leads to

\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z-\Delta_{i}^{n}Z^{c}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|\leq u_{n},\,|\Delta_{i}^{n}Z|>u_{n},\,\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\circ}\|\leq u_{n}\}}\right]=O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r}\vee\Delta_{n}^{2}u_{n}^{-2r}),

(A.340)

and thus
Eq.

A.323
holds. Combining
Eqs.

A.322
and
A.323
in Eqs. (
A.321
) and (
A.317
), we obtain

\|(\mathbf{P}_{j}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{Z}^{(3)}\|^{2}=O_{p}(u_{n}^{2-r}\vee\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-2r})=o_{p}(\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)),

(A.341)

when
\Delta_{n}^{(1-\varpi(2-r))\vee 2\varpi r}(K_{n}+\log p)\to\infty
as required.

(iii)
\bm{\Xi=\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{R}_{A^{\circ}}\bm{\gamma}_{A^{\circ}}}

Consider the same decomposition as in Appendix
A.3
(
i
), i.e.,
Eqs.

A.71
and
A.72
,

\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{R}_{A^{\circ}}\bm{\gamma}_{A^{\circ}}=(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{R}_{A^{\circ}}\bm{\gamma}_{A^{\circ}})+\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(2)}.

(A.342)

Since the oracle index set
A^{\circ}
has fixed cardinality
q
, the arguments used in the proofs of
Theorems

1
and
2
apply verbatim to the restricted oracle regression. By
Eqs.

A.74
,
A.75
and
A.76
and Markov’s inequality,

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{R}_{A^{\circ}}\bm{\gamma}_{A^{\circ}}\|^{2}=O_{p}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r}\vee u_{n}^{2-r})=O_{p}(u_{n}^{2-r}).

(A.343)

and, by
Eq.

A.79
,

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{(2)}\|^{2}=O_{p}(\Delta_{n}^{2}K_{n}^{2}).

(A.344)

Combining
Eqs.

A.343
and
A.344
in
Eq.

A.342
leads to

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{R}_{A^{\circ}}\bm{\gamma}_{A^{\circ}}\|^{2}=O_{p}(u_{n}^{2-r}\vee\Delta_{n}^{2}K_{n}^{2})=o_{p}(\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)),

(A.345)

when
\Delta_{n}^{1-\varpi(2-r)}(K_{n}+\log p)\to\infty
, and since
\Delta_{n}K_{n}\to 0
,

\Delta_{n}^{2}K_{n}^{2}=o(\Delta_{n}K_{n})=o(\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)).

(A.346)

(iv)
\bm{\Xi=\mathbf{U}^{(2)}}

By Hölder’s and BDG inequalities, as well as
Lemma

A.2
(i), for any
q\geq 2
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{U}_{i}^{2}]&=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n}\}}\right]\\
&\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{n}}^{i\Delta_{n}}e_{s}^{\top}dX_{s}^{c}\right)^{2q}\right]\right)^{1/q}\left(\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y|>u_{n})\right)^{1-1/q}\\
&\leq K\Delta_{n}(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-r})^{1-1/q}=K\Delta_{n}^{2-1/q}u_{n}^{r/q-r},\end{split}

(A.347)

and it is
O(\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{2-r})
if we choose
q\geq(1-\varpi r)/(1-2\varpi)
, which is always possible for
1/2(2-r)<\varpi<1/2
and
0\leq r<1
. Therefore,

\|\mathbf{U}\|^{2}=O_{p}(u_{n}^{2-r})=o_{p}(\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)),

(A.348)

when
\Delta_{n}^{1-\varpi(2-r)}(K_{n}+\log p)\to\infty
.

(v)
\bm{\Xi=\mathbf{J}}

Same as in (
ii
), we employ the safe replacement
\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}
with the additional truncation indicator
\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\circ}\|\leq u_{n}\}}
. By
Eq.

A.97
and Markov’s inequality,

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}\|^{2}=O_{p}(u_{n}^{2-r}\vee\Delta_{n}u_{n}^{-2r})=o_{p}(\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)),

(A.349)

when
\Delta_{n}^{(1-\varpi(2-r))\vee 2\varpi r}(K_{n}+\log p)\to\infty
.

Combining results in (
i
)–(
v
) in
Eq.

A.317
completes the proof of
Lemma

A.5
.

∎

For any index set
A
, we define the total loss reduction from adding all blocks in
A\backslash A^{\circ}
as

\Delta Q_{n}(A)=Q_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})-\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma}).

(A.350)

Lemma A.6
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

5
, it holds that for any
A\in\Gamma_{1}
,

\Delta Q_{n}(A)\leq(|A|-q)\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\Delta Q_{n,j}

(A.351)

Proof of
Lemma

A.6
.

We denote by
Q_{n}(A)=\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})
, where
Q_{n}(A^{\circ})=Q_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})
. Fix
A\in\Gamma_{1}
, and enumerate the redundant groups as
A\backslash A^{\circ}=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{|A|-q}\}
. Define the nested sequence of index sets

\overline{A}_{0}=A^{\circ},\qquad\overline{A}_{k}=A^{\circ}\cup\{j_{1},\dots,j_{k}\},\qquad\text{where }k=1,\dots,|A|-q.

(A.352)

The total improvement from
A^{\circ}
to
A
can be written as a sum of incremental improvements:

\Delta Q_{n}(A)=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}-\mathbf{M}_{A})\mathbf{Y}=\sum_{k=1}^{|A|-q}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{\overline{A}_{k-1}}-\mathbf{M}_{\overline{A}_{k}})\mathbf{Y}.

(A.353)

We define the residualized block
\breve{\mathbf{R}}_{j_{k}}^{*}=\mathbf{M}_{\overline{A}_{k-1}}\mathbf{R}_{j_{k}}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times K_{n}}
, the orthogonal projection matrix
\breve{\mathbf{H}}_{j_{k}}^{*}=\breve{\mathbf{R}}_{j_{k}}^{*}((\breve{\mathbf{R}}_{j_{k}}^{*})^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{R}}_{j_{k}}^{*})^{-1}(\breve{\mathbf{R}}_{j_{k}}^{*})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
. We follow the same steps as in
Eqs.

A.298
and
A.299
, by the Frisch-Waugh-Lovell theorem,

\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{\overline{A}_{k-1}}-\mathbf{M}_{\overline{A}_{k}})\mathbf{Y}=(\mathbf{M}_{\overline{A}_{k-1}}\mathbf{Y})^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{H}}_{j_{k}}^{*}(\mathbf{M}_{\overline{A}_{k-1}}\mathbf{Y})=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{H}}_{j_{k}}^{*}\mathbf{Y}.

(A.354)

Since
A^{\circ}\subseteq\overline{A}_{k-1}
for all
k=1,\dots,|A|-q
,
\text{span}(\breve{\mathbf{R}}_{j_{k}}^{*})=\text{span}(\mathbf{M}_{\overline{A}_{k-1}}\mathbf{R}_{j_{k}}^{*})\subseteq\text{span}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{R}_{j_{k}}^{*})
, and thus the projector
\breve{\mathbf{H}}_{j_{k}}^{*}
is onto a smaller subspace than
\mathbf{H}_{j_{k}}^{*}
, so that

\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\breve{\mathbf{H}}_{j_{k}}^{*}\mathbf{Y}\leq\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{j_{k}}^{*}\mathbf{Y},\qquad\text{where}\quad\mathbf{H}_{j_{k}}^{*}=(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{R}_{j_{k}}^{*})((\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{R}_{j_{k}}^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{R}_{j_{k}}^{*})^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}\mathbf{R}_{j_{k}}^{*}.

(A.355)

Therefore, by the definition of
\Delta Q_{n,j}
in
Eq.

A.296
and the same steps as in
Eq.

A.354
,

\Delta Q_{n}(A)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{|A|-q}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{j_{k}}^{*}\mathbf{Y}\leq(|A|-q)\max_{q+1\leq j\leq p}\Delta Q_{n,j}.

(A.356)

This completes the proof of
Lemma

A.6
.

∎

By
Lemmas

A.5
and
A.6
, it holds for any
A\in\Gamma_{1}
on the event
E_{n}
in
Eq.

A.297
that

\Delta Q_{n}(A)\leq K(|A|-q)\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p).

(A.357)

By
Eqs.

A.295
and
A.357
, on
E_{n}
,

\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})-Q_{n}^{*}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})\geq(|A|-q)\left(-\frac{K}{2}\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)+\lambda_{n}\right)>0,

(A.358)

since
|A|-q\geq 1
and
\lambda_{n}\gg\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)
as required. Consequently, by
Lemma

A.5
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\exists A\in\Gamma_{1}:\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})-Q_{n}^{*}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})\leq 0\right)\leq\mathbb{P}(E_{n}^{\complement})\to 0.

(A.359)

Underfitting models

For any underfitting models with
A\in\Gamma_{2}
, the difference between unpenalized losses in
Eq.

A.295
satisfies

Q_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})-\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}-\mathbf{M}_{A})\mathbf{Y},

(A.360)

where the right-hand side corresponds to the decrease of OLS residual sums of squares from adding missed relevant regressors in
A^{\prime\prime}=A^{\circ}\backslash A\neq\varnothing
to
A
.

Consider the decomposition of the design matrix
\mathbf{R}=(\mathbf{R}_{A}^{\top},\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\top},\mathbf{R}_{\{1,\dots,p\}\backslash A^{\circ}}^{\top})^{\top}
and the oracle estimator
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ}=((\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A}^{\circ})^{\top},(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ})^{\top},0^{\top})^{\top}
, and thus

\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{M}_{A}(\mathbf{R}_{A}\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A}^{\circ}+\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}+\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}})=\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}+\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}},

(A.361)

because
\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{R}_{A}=0
and
\mathbf{M}_{A}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}=\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}
by definition. With similar applications of the Frisch-Waugh-Lovell theorem as in
Eq.

A.298
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}})\mathbf{Y}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A}-\mathbf{M}_{A\cup A^{\prime\prime}})\mathbf{Y}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{Y})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}((\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}})^{-1}(\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{Y}

\displaystyle=(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}+2(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}+\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}})^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\geq(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}+2(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}},

(A.362)

where the nonnegative quadratic term of
\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}
is dropped for a lower bound. Since
\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}
is orthogonal to the span of
\mathbf{R}_{A^{\circ}}
, we have
\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\varepsilon}}=\bm{0}
and thus the cross term vanishes. Therefore, it holds that

\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}})\mathbf{Y}\geq(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{A}\mathbf{R}_{A^{\prime\prime}}\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}\geq K\sum_{j\in A^{\prime\prime}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}\geq K(q-|A|)\min_{j\in A^{\prime\prime}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}.

(A.363)

By
Eqs.

A.258
and
A.259
,
\sqrt{(\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\|\beta_{j}\|_{L^{2}}>0
for all
j\in A^{\circ}
.
Therefore, for some
b_{\text{min}}=K\min_{j\in A^{\circ}}\|\beta_{j}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}>0
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{j\in A^{\circ}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}\geq b_{\text{min}}\right)\to 1.

(A.364)

By
Eqs.

A.295
and
A.363
,

\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})-Q_{n}^{*}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})\geq\frac{K}{2}|A^{\prime\prime}|\min_{j\in A^{\prime\prime}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}-\lambda_{n}|A^{\prime\prime}|.

(A.365)

With the union bound in
Eq.

A.364
and
\lambda_{n}\to 0
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}\left(\exists A\in\Gamma_{2}:\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})-Q_{n}^{*}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})\leq 0\right)\leq 1-\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{j\in A^{\circ}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j}\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{\circ}\geq b_{\text{min}}\right)\to 0.

(A.366)

Misspecified models

For any misspecified models with
A\in\Gamma_{3}
, we denote by
A^{\prime}=A\cap A^{\circ}
the index set of included relevant regressors, by
A^{\prime\prime}=A^{\circ}\backslash A\neq\varnothing
the index set of missed relevant regressors, and by
A^{\prime\prime\prime}=A\backslash A^{\circ}\neq\varnothing
the index set of spurious regressors, and thus
A^{\circ}=A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}
and
A=A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}
. It holds that

\begin{split}Q_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})-\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})&=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}-\mathbf{M}_{A})\mathbf{Y}\\
&=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}})\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}})\mathbf{Y}\\
&=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}})\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}})\mathbf{Y},\end{split}

(A.367)

where the first term is the decrease of OLS residual sums of squares from adding missed relevant regressors, and the second term is the increase from excluding spurious regressors. For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}})\mathbf{Y}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}}+\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}})\mathbf{Y}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}})\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}})\mathbf{Y},

(A.368)

since
\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}})\mathbf{Y}\leq 0
, and therefore

Q_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})-\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})\leq\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}})\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}}-\mathbf{M}_{A^{\circ}\cup A^{\prime\prime\prime}})\mathbf{Y}.

(A.369)

By
Lemmas

A.5
and
A.6
, it holds that, on the event
E_{n}
,

Q_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})-\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}(\bm{\gamma})\leq K|A^{\prime\prime\prime}|\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p).

(A.370)

Bring it back to
Eq.

A.295
, on the event
E_{n}
,

\begin{split}\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})-Q_{n}^{*}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})&\geq-\frac{K}{2}|A^{\prime\prime\prime}|\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)+\lambda_{n}(|A^{\prime}|+|A^{\prime\prime\prime}|-q)\\
&\geq|A^{\prime\prime\prime}|\left(-\frac{K}{2}\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)+\lambda_{n}\right)-\lambda_{n}|A^{\prime\prime}|>0,\end{split}

(A.371)

since
|A^{\prime\prime\prime}|\geq 1
,
\lambda_{n}\gg\Delta_{n}(K_{n}+\log p)
, and
\lambda_{n}\to 0
. Consequently, by
Lemma

A.5
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\exists A\in\Gamma_{3}:\min_{\bm{\gamma}:A(\bm{\gamma})=A}Q_{n}^{*}(\bm{\gamma})-Q_{n}^{*}(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{\circ})\leq 0\right)\leq\mathbb{P}(E_{n}^{\complement})\to 0.

(A.372)

In conclusion,
Eqs.

A.359
,
A.366
and
A.372
are sufficient for
Eq.

A.293
. This completes the proof.

Appendix B
Supplementary Materials

B.1
Supplementary Simulation Results

In addition to the simulation results in
Section

4
(
Tables

1
and
2
), this appendix reports supplementary Monte Carlo evidence on the sensitivity of the proposed estimation procedure. Unless stated otherwise, the data-generating process and tuning choices follow the baseline design in
Section

4.1
, and results are reported in
Table

B.1
–
B.4
.

Alternative truncation threshold

Tables

B.1
and
B.2
report the model estimation and selection results, respectively, with a less aggressive truncation threshold
4\Delta_{n}^{0.47}\sqrt{\text{MedRV}_{T}}
for the increments of
Y
and each component of
X
, with the same simulation design used in
Section

4
.

Imperfect multicollinearity

To introduce imperfect multicollinearity while preserving the baseline dependence among the relevant factors, we construct the
p\times p
correlation matrix in block form. We fix the relevant block to have the same Toeplitz structure as in
Section

4.1
, i.e.,

\bm{\rho}_{11}=\left(0.15^{|i-j|}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq q}.

(B.1)

We then generate the redundant-factor block
\bm{\rho}_{22}
from a simple latent-factor construction, which is positive definite by construction and can exhibit relatively strong within-block dependence. Specifically, we draw a loading matrix
\textbf{L}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p-q)\times k}
, where
k
is the latent-factor dimension, and set

\bm{\rho}_{22}=\textbf{L}\textbf{L}^{\top}+\mathrm{diag}(1-\|\textbf{L}_{i\cdot}\|^{2}),

(B.2)

where
\|\textbf{L}_{i\cdot}\|
is the Euclidean norm of the
i
-th row of
L
. Next, we draw a candidate cross-correlation block
\bm{\rho}_{12}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times(p-q)}
with entries i.i.d.
U[-1,1]
. Since an arbitrary choice of
\bm{\rho}_{12}
may violate positive definiteness of the full matrix, we scale the cross-block by a single factor and set
\bm{\rho}_{12}=\kappa\bm{\rho}_{12}^{*}
, where
\kappa\in(0,1]
is chosen as large as possible subject to the Schur-complement condition, i.e.,

\bm{\rho}_{22}-\kappa^{2}(\bm{\rho}_{12}^{*})^{\top}\bm{\rho}_{11}^{-1}\bm{\rho}_{12}^{*}

(B.3)

is positive definite. The final correlation matrix

\bm{\rho}=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{\rho}_{11}&\bm{\rho}_{12}\\
\bm{\rho}_{12}^{\top}&\bm{\rho}_{22}\end{pmatrix}

(B.4)

is symmetric with unit diagonal and positive definite. Hence, the relevant-factor dependence remains comparable to the baseline design in the main text, while it allows stronger dependence among the redundant factors and nontrivial correlations between relevant and redundant factors.
Fig.

B.1
plots the histogram of all off-diagonal entries of
\bm{\rho}
for the case
p=500
. The model estimation and selection results are reported in
Tables

B.3
and
B.4
, respectively.

Figure B.1:
Histogram of all off-diagonal entries of the correlation matrix
\bm{\rho}
for the case
p=500
.

Average number of selected factors per year for all selected S&P 100 stocks. The candidate covariate set consists of the 224 high-frequency factors of
Aleti, 2023
. The cutoff level in the TLP function is
\tau_{n}=0.01\sqrt{\text{MedRV}_{T}}
, where
\mathrm{MedRV}_{T}
denotes the MedRV of the test asset computed over the corresponding estimation window
[0,T]
. The number of B-spline basis functions
K_{n}
and the effective penalty level
\lambda_{n}/\tau_{n}
are selected via 5-fold cross-validation using the one-standard-error rule.

. The cutoff level in the TLP function is
